For at the pistol shot ’twas as if a mask of horror had fallen from his face, and left him his old countenance again; and then I saw he turned his eyes towards the cliff-top, and thought that he was looking up in thankfulness to heaven.

===Chinese===

我随即放开了他，踉跄了片刻——那一阵缠斗教我乏透了，心里却还欢喜，因为我看见，便是这样短的一线喘息，也给 Maskew 带来了那样的安宁。枪声一响，仿佛一张惊怖的面具从他脸上脱落了下去，还给他本来的一副面容；随后我见他朝崖顶转过眼睛，只当他是在仰面感谢上天。

===Original===

But now a new thing happened; for before the echoes of that pistol-shot had died on the keen morning air, I thought I heard a noise of distant shouting, and looked about to see whence it could come. Elzevir looked round too, forgetting to upbraid me for making him miss his aim, but Maskew still kept his face turned up towards the cliff. Then the voices came nearer, and there was a mingled sound as of men shouting to one another, and gathering in from different places. ’Twas from the cliff-top that the voices came, and thither Elzevir and I looked up, and there too Maskew kept his eyes fixed. And in a moment there were a score of men stood on the cliff’s edge high above our heads. The sky behind them was pink flushed with the keenest light of the young day, and they stood out against it sharp cut and black as the silhouette of my mother that used to hang up by the parlour chimney. They were soldiers, and I knew the tall mitre-caps of the 13th, and saw the shafts of light from the sunrise come flashing round their bodies, and glance off the barrels of their matchlocks.

===Chinese===

可就在这时，新的变故来了：那一声枪响的回声还没在凛冽的晨气里散尽，我就仿佛听得远处一片呐喊，四下张望，想看声音会打哪里来。Elzevir 也回头看，忘了斥责我坏他的瞄准；Maskew 却仍旧仰着脸朝着崖顶。接着声音近了，乱纷纷响成一片，像许多人互相呼喊，从各处围拢过来。声音正是打崖顶上下来的；Elzevir 和我朝那里仰望，Maskew 的眼睛也钉在那里。顷刻之间，我们头顶高高的崖沿上站了二十来个人。他们背后的天，让新的一天最锐亮的光浸成一片绯红；他们衬在那上面，轮廓分明、黑黢黢的，正像从前挂在我家客厅烟囱旁边的那幅我母亲的剪影。那是兵；我认得第十三团那高高的主教帽式军帽，看得见日出的一道道光在他们身子四周闪耀，又从他们火绳枪的枪管上反射开去。

===Original===

I knew it all now; it was the Posse who had lain in ambush. Elzevir saw it too, and then all shouted at once. “Yield at the King’s command: you are our prisoners!” calls the voice of one of those black silhouettes, far up on the cliff-top.

===Chinese===

这一下我全明白了：原来是缉私队设下了埋伏。Elzevir 也看出来了；跟着众人一齐呐喊。“奉国王之命投降：你们已经是我们的俘虏了！”高高的崖顶上，一个黑影里的声音喊道。

===Original===

“We are lost,” cries Elzevir; “it is the Posse; but if we die, this traitor shall go before us,” and he makes towards Maskew to brain him with the pistol.

===Chinese===

“我们完了，”Elzevir 喊道，“是缉私队！可要是死，也得叫这奸贼死在我们前头。”说着他直奔 Maskew，要抡起手枪砸碎他的脑袋。

===Original===

“Shoot, shoot, in the Devil’s name,” screams Maskew, “or I am a dead man.”

===Chinese===

“开枪，开枪，看在魔鬼份上！”Maskew 尖声叫喊，“不然我就死定了。”

===Original===

Then there came a flash of fire along the black line of silhouettes, with a crackle like a near peal of thunder, and a fut, fut, fut, of bullets in the turf. And before Elzevir could get at him, Maskew had fallen over on the sward with a groan, and with a little red hole in the middle of his forehead.

===Chinese===

随即那一线黑影里腾起一道火光，炸响得像一阵近处的霹雳，子弹噗、噗、噗地钻进草皮。Elzevir 还没够着 Maskew，他已呻吟一声，扑倒在了草地上，额头正中留着一个小小的红洞。

===Original===

“Run for the cliff-side,” cried Elzevir to me; “get close in, and they cannot touch thee,” and he made for the chalk wall. But I had fallen on my knees like a bullock felled by a poleaxe, and had a scorching pain in my left foot. Elzevir looked back. “What, have they hit thee too?” he said, and ran and picked me up like a child. And then there is another flash and fut, fut, in the turf; but the shots find no billet this time, and we are lying close against the cliff, panting but safe.


===Chinese===

“往崖根跑，”Elzevir 朝我喊，“贴紧了，他们就打不着你！”说着他直奔那面白垩的崖墙。可我已经双膝跪倒，像一头叫屠斧放翻的公牛，左脚上一阵火辣辣的灼痛。Elzevir 回过头来。“怎么，他们也打中你了？”他说着跑回来，像抱一个孩子似的把我抱起。这时又是一道火光，又是噗、噗两声打进草皮；这一回枪弹再没找着归宿，我们紧贴着崖壁躺着，喘着气，却安然无恙。

## X The Escape / 十 逃亡

===Original===

“… How fearful
						

						And dizzy ’tis to cast one’s eyes so low!
						

						… I’ll look no more
						

						Lest my brain turn.”

> Shakespeare

===Chinese===

“……就这样向下望一眼，
便叫人何等心惊目眩！
……我不要再看了，
免得头脑也跟着翻转。”

> Shakespeare

===Original===

The white chalk was a bulwark between us and the foe; and though one or two of them loosed off their matchlocks, trying to get at us sideways, they could not even see their quarry, and ’twas only shooting at a venture. We were safe. But for how short a time! Safe just for so long as it should please the soldiers not to come down to take us, safe with a discharged pistol in our grasp, and a shot man lying at our feet.

===Chinese===

白垩的崖壁成了我们和追兵之间的一道屏障；他们中间虽有一两个放松了火绳枪，想侧着身子够到我们，却连猎物的影子也望不见，那不过是瞎放一气罢了。我们安全了。可这安全又有多短暂！不过趁士兵们还没下来捉拿我们、趁我们手里只握着一杆打空了的手枪、脚边还躺着一个中了枪的人——不过这么一小会儿的安全罢了。

===Original===

Elzevir was the first to speak: “Can you stand, John? Is the bone broken?”

===Chinese===

Elzevir 先开了口：“你还站得住么，John？骨头断了没有？”

===Original===

“I cannot stand,” I said; “there is something gone in my leg, and I feel blood running down into my boot.”

===Chinese===

“我站不起来了，”我说，“腿里像丢了点什么，只觉得血正顺着往下流进靴子里。”

===Original===

He knelt, and rolled down the leg of my stocking; but though he only moved my foot ever so little, it caused me sharp pain, for feeling was coming back after the first numbness of the shot.

===Chinese===

他跪下来，把我袜筒卷下去；可他只消把我的脚挪动那么一丁点儿，也叫我钻心地疼，因为枪伤起初的麻木过去之后，痛觉正一点点醒回来。

===Original===

“They have broke the leg, though it bleeds little,” Elzevir said. “We have no time to splice it here, but I will put a kerchief round, and while I wrap it, listen to how we lie, and then choose what we shall do.”

===Chinese===

“腿骨断了，好在血淌得不多，”Elzevir 说，“眼下没工夫在这儿接骨，我先拿一条手巾给你扎上。趁我包扎的当儿，你听听咱们的处境，然后再挑怎么办。”

===Original===

I nodded, biting my lips hard to conceal the pain he gave me, and he went on: “We have a quarter of an hour before the Posse can get down to us. But come they will, and thou canst judge what chance we have to save liberty or life with that carrion lying by us”﻿—and he jerked his thumb at Maskew﻿—“though I am glad ’twas not my hand that sent him to his reckoning, and therefore do not blame thee if thou didst make me waste a charge in air. So one thing we can do is to wait here until they come, and I can account for a few of them before they shoot me down; but thou canst not fight with a broken leg, and they will take thee alive, and then there is a dance on air at Dorchester Jail.”

===Chinese===

我点点头，狠狠咬住嘴唇，忍住他弄出来的疼痛；他接着说：“缉私队赶到我们这儿，还有一刻钟的工夫。可他们一定会来；身边躺着这么一具臭尸，咱们保命保自由还有几分指望，你自己也掂量得出。”说着他朝 Maskew 那边扬了扬大拇指——“虽说送他去见阎王的不曾是我的手，我心里倒也痛快；所以你害我把一膛火药空放上天，我也不怪你。咱们能做的一件事，就是在这儿等他们来，趁他们把我打倒之前，先送掉他们几个；可是你拖着一条断腿没法打仗，他们会活捉了你，到那时就等着在多切斯特监狱里‘悬空跳舞’吧。”

===Original===

I felt sick with pain and bitterly cast down to think that I was like to come so soon to such a vile end; so only gave a sigh, wishing heartily that Maskew were not dead, and that my leg were not broke, but that I was back again at the Why Not? or even hearing one of Dr. Sherlock’s sermons in my aunt’s parlour.

===Chinese===

我疼得发晕，一想到自己这么快就要落得这样一个下场，心里凄苦到了极处；我只叹了一口气，满心盼望 Maskew 没有死、我的腿没有断，盼望我又回到了“Why Not?”里，哪怕是在姑妈的客厅里听 Sherlock 博士讲道也好。

===Original===

Elzevir looked down at me when I sighed, and seeing, I suppose, that I was sorrowful, tried to put a better face on a bad business. “Forgive me, lad,” he said, “if I have spoke too roughly. There is yet another way that we may try; and if thou hadst but two whole legs, I would have tried it, but now ’tis little short of madness. And yet, if thou fear’st not, I will still try it. Just at the end of this flat ledge, farthest from where the bridle-path leads down, but not a hundred yards from where we stand, there is a sheep-track leading up the cliff. It starts where the under-cliff dies back again into the chalk face, and climbs by slants and elbow-turns up to the top. The shepherds call it the Zigzag, and even sheep lose their footing on it; and of men I never heard but one had climbed it, and that was lander Jordan, when the Excise was on his heels, half a century back. But he that tries it stakes all on head and foot, and a wounded bird like thee may not dare that flight. Yet, if thou art content to hang thy life upon a hair, I will carry thee some way; and where there is no room to carry, thou must down on hands and knees and trail thy foot.”

===Chinese===

我叹气的时候，Elzevir 低头看了看我，大概是见我神色凄惶，便想给这桩糟糕的买卖添几分亮色。“原谅我，孩子，”他说，“要是我方才话说得太重。咱们还有一条路可以一试；你若长着两条好腿，我早试了，可眼下这法子同发疯也差不了多少。话虽如此，你若不怕，我仍旧要试它一试。就在这道平台的尽头，离驮马小径下坡的地方最远、离咱们站的地方却不出一百码，有一条羊踩出来的小道通往崖顶。它起在崖下台地重新缩回白垩崖壁的地方，顺着斜坡和肘弯一道道拐上去，直通崖顶。牧羊人管它叫“之字径”（the Zigzag），连羊在上面都要失蹄；人里头我只听说有一个爬上去过，那就是接货人 Jordan（lander Jordan），五十年前税关追到他脚跟的时候。只是走这条路的人，全把性命押在脑袋和脚上；像你这样一只受伤的鸟，未必敢飞这一遭。不过，你要是甘愿把命悬在一根头发丝上，我可以背你走一程；背不过去的地方，你就得手脚并用趴着爬，把那条脚拖在后头。”

===Original===

It was a desperate chance enough, but came as welcome as a patch of blue through lowering skies. “Yes,” I said, “dear Master Elzevir, let us get to it quickly; and if we fall, ’tis better far to die upon the rocks below than to wait here for them to hale us off to jail.” And with that I tried to stand, thinking I might go dot and carry even with a broken leg. But ’twas no use, and down I sank with a groan. Then Elzevir caught me up, holding me in his arms, with my head looking over his back, and made off for the Zigzag. And as we slunk along, close to the cliff-side, I saw, between the brambles, Maskew lying with his face turned up to the morning sky. And there was the little red hole in the middle of his forehead, and a thread of blood that welled up from it and trickled off on to the sward.

===Chinese===

这分明是九死一生的赌注，可它来得就像阴沉的天幕上裂开的一线蓝天，叫人求之不得。“好，”我说，“亲爱的 Elzevir 师傅，咱们快去吧；就算摔下去，摔死在下边的岩石上，也远比待在这儿等他们拖去坐牢强得多。”说着我便想站起来，心想哪怕断着一条腿，一瘸一拐也能走。可这全是白费，我哼了一声又瘫了下去。于是 Elzevir 一把抱起我，把我搂在怀里，让我的头从他背后探出去，直奔之字径而去。我们贴着崖根悄悄溜过去时，我从荆棘的缝隙里望见 Maskew 仰面躺在那儿，脸朝着清晨的天空。他额头正中是那个小小的红洞，一线血从洞里渗出来，涓涓淌到草地上。

===Original===

It was a sight to stagger any man, and would have made me swoon perhaps, but that there was no time, for we were at the end of the under-cliff, and Elzevir set me down for a minute, before he buckled to his task. And ’twas a task that might cow the bravest, and when I looked upon the Zigzag, it seemed better to stay where we were and fall into the hands of the Posse than set foot on that awful way, and fall upon the rocks below. For the Zigzag started off as a fair enough chalk path, but in a few paces narrowed down till it was but a whiter thread against the grey-white cliff-face, and afterwards turned sharply back, crossing a hundred feet direct above our heads. And then I smelt an evil stench, and looking about, saw the blown-out carcass of a rotting sheep lie close at hand.

===Chinese===

这景象足以叫任何一个汉子腿软，若在平时我多半要晕过去，只是眼下没有那个工夫，因为我们已到了崖下台地的尽头，Elzevir 把我放下来歇一口气，再着手他的营生。那营生是再勇的人见了也要胆寒的；我朝之字径一望，只觉得与其把脚踏上那条可怕的路、摔死在下边的岩石上，还不如原地不动，落进缉私队手里算了。原来那之字径起头还算一条像样的白垩小径，可没走几步就窄下去，窄到在灰白的崖壁上只剩一条稍白些的细线，随后猛地折回来，从我们头顶正上方一百英尺的地方横穿过去。这时我闻到一股恶臭，四下一看，只见一头胀得鼓鼓、腐烂了的羊尸躺在近旁。

===Original===

“Faugh,” said Elzevir, “ ’tis a poor beast has lost his foothold.”

===Chinese===

“呸，”Elzevir 说，“是一头可怜的畜生失了蹄。”

===Original===

It was an ill omen enough, and I said as much, beseeching him to make his own way up the Zigzag and leave me where I was, for that they might have mercy on a boy.

===Chinese===

这不是个好兆头，我也这么说了，恳求他独自上之字径去，把我留在原地，因为他们兴许会饶过一个孩子。

===Original===

“Tush!” he cried; “it is thy heart that fails thee, and ’tis too late now to change counsel. We have fifteen minutes yet to win or lose with, and if we gain the cliff-top in that time we shall have an hour’s start, or more, for they will take all that to search the under-cliff. And Maskew, too, will keep them in check a little, while they try to bring the life back to so good a man. But if we fall, why, we shall fall together, and outwit their cunning. So shut thy eyes, and keep them tight until I bid thee open them.” With that he caught me up again, and I shut my eyes firm, rebuking myself for my faint-heartedness, and not telling him how much my foot hurt me. In a minute I knew from Elzevir’s steps that he had left the turf and was upon the chalk. Now I do not believe that there were half a dozen men beside in England who would have ventured up that path, even free and untrammelled, and not a man in all the world to do it with a full-grown lad in his arms. Yet Elzevir made no bones of it, nor spoke a single word; only he went very slow, and I felt him scuffle with his foot as he set it forward, to make sure he was putting it down firm.

===Chinese===

“啐！”他喝道，“是你自己心虚了；如今再改主意也晚了。咱们还有一刻钟好赢好输，若能在这工夫里抢上崖顶，就能先他们一个钟头，还不止；因为光搜这片崖下台地，就够他们忙那半天。再说 Maskew 也能牵住他们一阵，他们总得想法子把这么一位‘好人’救活呀。可要是摔下去，好啊，咱们就摔作一堆，叫他们的奸计落个空。所以闭上眼睛，闭得紧紧的，等我叫你睁再睁。”说罢他又抱起我，我把眼睛闭得死紧，一面在心里责备自己不该这样懦怯，也没有告诉他我的脚疼得多么厉害。过了一分钟，我从 Elzevir 的脚步里听出他已离开草皮、踏上了白垩。我敢说，全英格兰除了他，再没有半打人肯上那条路去冒险，哪怕空着身子、无牵无碍；至于抱着一个半大小子走上去，普天之下更没有第二个人办得到。可 Elzevir 毫不迟疑，一句话也不说，只是走得极慢；我觉出他每挪一步，脚底都要蹭上一蹭，好确定踩实了才敢落下去。

===Original===

I said nothing, not wishing to distract him from his terrible task, and held my breath, when I could, so that I might lie quieter in his arms. Thus he went on for a time that seemed without end, and yet was really but a minute or two; and by degrees I felt the wind, that we could scarce perceive at all on the under-cliff, blow fresher and cold on the cliff-side. And then the path grew steeper and steeper, and Elzevir went slower and slower, till at last he spoke:

===Chinese===

我一声不吭，生怕搅扰了他那可怕的营生，还屏住呼吸——屏得住的时候——好在他怀里躺得安稳些。他就这样走下去，那段时间仿佛没有尽头，其实不过一两分钟；渐渐地，我觉出风来了——在崖下台地上我们几乎察觉不到的风——在崖壁上越吹越劲、越吹越冷。接着路越来越陡，Elzevir 越走越慢，末了他终于开口：

===Original===

“John, I am going to stop; but open not thy eyes till I have set thee down and bid thee.”

===Chinese===

“John，我要停一停了；可在我把你放下、吩咐你之前，先别睁眼。”

===Original===

I did as bidden, and he lowered me gently, setting me on all-fours upon the path; and speaking again:

===Chinese===

我依言而行，他轻轻把我放低，让我手脚伏在那条小径上；又开口道：

===Original===

“The path is too narrow here for me to carry thee, and thou must creep round this corner on thy hands and knees. But have a care to keep thy outer hand near to the inner, and the balance of thy body to the cliff, for there is no room to dance hornpipes here. And hold thy eyes fixed on the chalk-wall, looking neither down nor seaward.”

===Chinese===

“这儿的小径太窄，我背不动你了，你得手脚并用，自己爬过这个弯。可要留神：外边那只手紧挨着里边的手，身子重心都靠着崖壁，这里可没有地方让你跳角笛舞。把眼睛钉在白垩崖壁上，既不要朝下看，也不要朝海看。”

===Original===

’Twas well he told me what to do, and well I did it; for when I opened my eyes, even without moving them from the cliff-side, I saw that the ledge was little more than a foot wide, and that ever so little a lean of the body would dash me on the rocks below. So I crept on, but spent much time that was so precious in travelling those ten yards to take me round the first elbow of the path; for my foot was heavy and gave me fierce pain to drag, though I tried to mask it from Elzevir. And he, forgetting what I suffered, cried out, “Quicken thy pace, lad, if thou canst, the time is short.” Now so frail is man’s temper, that though he was doing more than any ever did to save another’s life, and was all I had to trust to in the world; yet because he forgot my pain and bade me quicken, my choler rose, and I nearly gave him back an angry word, but thought better of it and kept it in.

===Chinese===

幸亏他先吩咐了我该怎么做，我也照办得妥当；因为我一睁眼，纵然眼珠不离崖壁，也看出那条石沿宽不过一英尺，身子只要往外偏那么一丝丝，就会把我摔碎在下边的岩石上。我便往前爬，可单是爬完那十码、绕过小径的头一个肘弯，就耗去了多少宝贵的时光；我的脚沉得厉害，一拖就疼得凶，虽然我竭力瞒过 Elzevir。可他把我的苦楚忘了，喊道：“快些，孩子，能快就快，时间不多了。”人的性子就是这样脆弱：他救人性命，做的事超乎古今一切人，又是我在世上唯一的倚靠；可只因他忘了我的疼、催我快些，我的火气便往上撞，险些回敬他一句狠话，幸而念头一转，又咽了回去。

===Original===

Then he told me to stop, for that the way grew wider and he would pick me up again. But here was another difficulty, for the path was still so narrow and the cliff-wall so close that he could not take me up in his arms. So I lay flat on my face, and he stepped over me, setting his foot between my shoulders to do it; and then, while he knelt down upon the path, I climbed up from behind upon him, putting my arms round his neck; and so he bore me “pickaback.” I shut my eyes firm again, and thus we moved along another spell, mounting still and feeling the wind still freshening.

===Chinese===

随后他叫我停下，说前路宽了些，要再背我起来。可这里又有一层难处：小径仍旧那样窄，崖壁又贴得那样近，他没法把我抱进怀里。我便脸朝下伏平了，他跨过我身上，一只脚踏在我两肩中间过去；然后他跪在小径上，我从背后爬上他的背，两条胳膊搂住他的脖子；他就这样“打驮”着了我。我又把眼睛闭得死紧，我们这样又走了一程，仍旧步步登高，风也越吹越劲。

===Original===

At length he said that we were come to the last turn of the path, and he must set me down once more. So down upon his knees and hands he went, and I slid off behind, on to the ledge. Both were on all-fours now; Elzevir first and I following. But as I crept along, I relaxed care for a moment, and my eyes wandered from the cliff-side and looked down. And far below I saw the blue sea twinkling like a dazzling mirror, and the gulls wheeling about the sheer chalk wall, and then I thought of that bloated carcass of a sheep that had fallen from this very spot perhaps, and in an instant felt a sickening qualm and swimming of the brain, and knew that I was giddy and must fall.

===Chinese===

末了他说，我们已到了小径最后一个弯，得再把我放下来。于是他手脚着地伏下去，我从背后溜下来，落在石沿上。这时我们两个都手脚并用了，Elzevir 在前，我在后。可我爬着爬着，一时松了小心，眼睛离开崖壁，朝下望了一眼。只见远远的下方，蓝色的海像一面晃眼的镜子闪着光，海鸥绕着笔直的白垩崖壁盘旋；我随即想起那头胀鼓鼓的羊尸——说不定正是从这个地方跌下去的——刹那间只觉一阵恶心翻上来，脑袋里天旋地转，我知道自己晕了，就要摔下去了。

===Original===

Then I called out to Elzevir, and he, guessing what had come over me, cries to turn upon my side, and press my belly to the cliff. And how he did it in such a narrow strait I know not; but he turned round, and lying down himself, thrust his hand firmly in my back, pressing me closer to the cliff. Yet it was none too soon, for if he had not held me tight, I should have flung myself down in sheer despair to get quit of that dreadful sickness.

===Chinese===

我喊了 Elzevir 一声，他一猜便知我出了什么毛病，叫我就地侧过身子，把肚皮贴紧崖壁。在那样窄的一条绝路上他是怎么转过身来的，我不知道；总之他转过来了，自己躺下，一只手死死抵住我的背，把我往崖壁上贴得更紧。也亏他来得及时，若不是他这样牢牢按住，我真会干脆绝望地纵身跳下去，好摆脱那可怕的晕眩。

===Original===

“Keep thine eyes shut, John,” he said, “and count up numbers loud to me, that I may know thou art not turning faint.” So I gave out, “One, two, three,” and while I went on counting, heard him repeating to himself, though his words seemed thin and far off: “We must have taken ten minutes to get here, and in five more they will be on the under-cliff; and if we ever reach the top, who knows but they have left a guard! No, no, they will not leave a guard, for not a man knows of the Zigzag; and, if they knew, they would not guess that we should try it. We have but fifty yards to go to win, and now this cursed giddy fit has come upon the child, and he will fall and drag me with him; or they will see us from below, and pick us off like sitting guillemots against the cliff-face.”

===Chinese===

“把眼睛闭住，John，”他说，“大声数数给我听，好叫我知道你没有发晕。”我便数出声来：“一，二，三……”我一路数下去，听见他自言自语，话音听起来又细又远：“到这儿怕是已花去十分钟，再过五分钟他们就要上崖下台地了；就算我们到得了崖顶，谁知道他们会不会留下哨！不，不会，他们不会留哨，因为没有一个人知道之字径；就算知道，也猜不到我们敢走它。再赢五十码就够了，偏偏这孩子该死地发起晕来，他一摔还要连我一起拖下去；再不然就是他们从底下望见我们，把我们当贴在崖壁上的呆海鸠，一只一只地打下来。”

===Original===

So he talked to himself, and all the while I would have given a world to pluck up heart and creep on farther; yet could not, for the deadly sweating fear that had hold of me. Thus I lay with my face to the cliff, and Elzevir pushing firmly in my back; and the thing that frightened me most was that there was nothing at all for the hand to take hold of, for had there been a piece of string, or even a thread of cotton, stretched along to give a semblance of support, I think I could have done it; but there was only the cliff-wall, sheer and white, against that narrowest way, with never cranny to put a finger into. The wind was blowing in fresh puffs, and though I did not open my eyes, I knew that it was moving the little tufts of bent grass, and the chiding cries of the gulls seemed to invite me to be done with fear and pain and broken leg, and fling myself off on to the rocks below.

===Chinese===

他这样自言自语着；这一路我情愿拿整个世界去换一点勇气，好壮起胆子再往前爬，却怎么也办不到，因为那冷汗淋漓的死怖还攥着我。我就这样脸贴着崖壁趴着，Elzevir 在背后死死抵住我；最叫我心惊的，是手里竟没有一样东西可以抓握——倘若有一根绳子，哪怕一根棉线，顺着路拉过去，装一装依靠的样子，我想我也能撑下来；可有的只是那笔直雪白的崖壁，贴着这条最窄的路，连一条能伸进手指的缝也没有。风一阵阵劲吹，我虽没睁眼，也知道它正掀动一小丛一小丛的硬草；海鸥那责骂似的啼声，仿佛在诱我把恐惧、疼痛和断腿一并做个了断，纵身跳到下面的岩石上去。

===Original===

Then Elzevir spoke. “John,” he said, “there is no time to play the woman; another minute of this and we are lost. Pluck up thy courage, keep thy eyes to the cliff, and forward.”

===Chinese===

这时 Elzevir 开口了。“John，”他说，“没有工夫学娘儿们了；再这么耗一分钟，我们就完了。鼓起勇气来，眼睛盯着崖壁，往前爬。”

===Original===

Yet I could not, but answered: “I cannot, I cannot; if I open my eyes, or move hand or foot, I shall fall on the rocks below.”

===Chinese===

可我办不到，只是答道：“不行，不行；我一睁眼、一动弹手脚，就要摔到下面的岩石上去了。”

===Original===

He waited a second, and then said: “Nay, move thou must, and ’tis better to risk falling now, than fall for certain with another bullet in thee later on.” And with that he shifted his hand from my back and fixed it in my coat-collar, moving backwards himself, and setting to drag me after him.

===Chinese===

他等了一秒，然后说：“不成，你非动不可；与其等他们追上来再挨一枪、稳稳当当摔下去，不如现在豁出去冒这一摔。”说着他把抵着我背的手挪开，攥住我的衣领，自己朝后退着走，拖着我跟上去。

===Original===

Now, I was so besotted with fright that I would not budge an inch, fearing to fall over if I opened my eyes. And Elzevir, for all he was so strong, could not pull a helpless lump backwards up that path. So he gave it up, leaving go hold on me with a groan, and at that moment there rose from the under-cliff, below a sound of voices and shouting.

===Chinese===

可我吓昏了头，一寸也不肯挪，生怕一睁眼就翻下去。Elzevir 纵有一身力气，也没法把这么一堆瘫软的死物倒拖着拽上那条路。他只好撒了手，叹一口气认了输；正在这当口，崖下台地那边升起一片人声和呐喊。

===Original===

“Zounds, they are down already!” cried Elzevir, “and have found Maskew’s body; it is all up; another minute and they will see us.”

===Chinese===

“天杀的，他们已经下来了！”Elzevir 失声喊道，“已经找着 Maskew 的尸首了；全完了；再过一分钟他们就要望见我们了。”

===Original===

But so strange is the force of mind on body, and the power of a greater to master a lesser fear, that when I heard those voices from below, all fright of falling left me in a moment, and I could open my eyes without a trace of giddiness. So I began to move forward again on hands and knees. And Elzevir, seeing me, thought for a moment I had gone mad, and was dragging myself over the cliff; but then saw how it was, and moved backwards himself before me, saying in a low voice, “Brave lad! Once creep round this turn, and I will pick thee up again. There is but fifty yards to go, and we shall foil these devils yet!”

===Chinese===

可心神对肉身的威力竟是这样奇妙，一种更大的恐惧竟能压服一种小些的恐惧：我一听见底下那些人声，怕摔的心思霎时丢了个干净，睁开眼睛，竟没有一丝晕意。我便又手脚并用地朝前爬起来。Elzevir 一见，有一瞬还当我发了疯，要自己滚下崖去；随即看出是怎么回事，便自己退到我前头，低声说：“好孩子！只要爬过这个弯，我就再背起你。不过再走五十码，咱们终究要叫这些魔鬼扑个空！”

===Original===

Then we heard the voices again, but farther off, and not so loud; and knew that our pursuers had left the under-cliff and turned down on to the beach, thinking that we were hiding by the sea.

===Chinese===

随后我们又听见那些人声，却远了，也低了；我们知道追兵已离开崖下台地，折向海滩下去了，只当我们藏在海边。

===Original===

Five minutes later Elzevir stepped on to the cliff-top, with me upon his back.

===Chinese===

五分钟后，Elzevir 踏上了崖顶，我还在他背上。

===Original===

“We have made something of this throw,” he said, “and are safe for another hour, though I thought thy giddy head had ruined us.”

===Chinese===

“这一把总算掷赢了，”他说，“咱们又保得一个钟头的平安——虽说你那晕晕的脑袋，方才险些把咱们全断送了。”

===Original===

Then he put me gently upon the springy turf, and lay down himself upon his back, stretching his arms out straight on either side, and breathing hard to recover from the task he had performed.

===Chinese===

随后他把我轻轻放在有弹性的草皮上，自己仰面躺倒，两条胳膊朝两边伸得笔直，喘着粗气，从那场劳役里缓过来。

===Original===

---

			

===Chinese===

---

===Original===

The day was still young, and far below us was stretched the moving floor of the Channel, with a silver-grey film of night-mists not yet lifted in the offing. A hummocky up-and-down line of cliffs, all projections, dents, bays, and hollows, trended southward till it ended in the great bluff of St. Alban’s Head, ten miles away. The cliff-face was gleaming white, the sea tawny inshore, but purest blue outside, with the straight sunpath across it, spangled and gleaming like a mackerel’s back.

===Chinese===

天色还早，我们脚下远远铺开海峡那面晃动的水面，远处海上一带夜雾还没散尽，蒙着银灰的薄膜。那道高低起伏、疙疙瘩瘩的崖线，满是凸角、凹湾、海湾和坑洼，一路向南延展，直到十英里外 St. Alban's Head 那座大断崖才收住。崖壁闪着亮白，近岸的海是黄褐色，外面却是极纯的蓝，一道笔直的日光铺在海上，像鲭鱼的脊背，斑斑点斑点着光。

===Original===

The relief of being once more on firm ground, and the exultation of an escape from immediate danger, removed my pain and made me forget that my leg was broken. So I lay for a moment basking in the sun; and the wind, which a few minutes before threatened to blow me from that narrow ledge, seemed now but the gentlest of breezes, fresh with the breath of the kindly sea. But this was only for a moment, for the anguish came back and grew apace, and I fell to thinking dismally of the plight we were in. How things had been against us in these last days! First there was losing the Why Not? and that was bad enough; second, there was the being known by the Excise for smugglers, and perhaps for murderers; third and last, there was the breaking of my leg, which made escape so difficult. But, most of all, there came before my eyes that grey face turned up against the morning sun, and I thought of all it meant for Grace, and would have given my own life to call back that of our worst enemy.

===Chinese===

重又踩上坚实的土地，那一份宽慰，加上死里逃生的狂喜，竟一时压住了疼，叫我忘了自己的腿已断了。我便躺着晒了一刻太阳；几分钟前还扬言要把我刮下那条窄石沿的风，此刻好像只剩最温柔的一缕，带着和善海面的清新气息。可这不过是一霎，剧痛很快卷土重来，越涨越凶，我沮沮丧丧地想起我们眼下的处境。这几天的事，桩桩都同我们作对！头一件是丢了“Why Not?”，那已经够糟了；第二件是税关把我们记成了走私贩，说不定还是杀人犯；第三件、也是末一件，是我断了的这条腿，把逃走弄得这样艰难。可最教我丢不开的，还是眼前浮起的那张灰白的脸——朝着朝阳仰着的那张脸；我想着这一切对 Grace 意味着什么，情愿拿自己的命去换回我们仇人里最狠的那一个的命。

===Original===

Then Elzevir sat up, stretching himself like one waking out of sleep, and said: “We must be gone. They will not be back for some time yet, and, when they come, will not think to search closely for us hereabouts; but that we cannot risk, and must get clear away. This leg of thine will keep us tied for weeks, and we must find some place where we can lie hid, and tend it. Now, I know such a hiding-hole in Purbeck, which they call Joseph’s Pit, and thither we must go; but it will take all the day to get there, for it is seven miles off, and I am older than I was, and thou too heavy a babe to carry over lightly.”

===Chinese===

随后 Elzevir 坐起身来，伸了个懒腰，像一个人从睡梦里醒来，说道：“咱们得走了。他们一时半会儿回不来；回来时也想不到在这一带细细搜我们；可这个险冒不得，非走远些不可。你这条腿要拖住咱们好几个星期，总得寻个地方藏身养伤。我知道珀贝克（Purbeck）有这么一个藏身的洞子，他们管它叫约瑟夫坑（Joseph's Pit）；咱们得上那儿去。只是到那儿要走上一整天，路有七英里，我上了年纪，不比当年；你这么个半大孩子，背起来也不轻省。”

===Original===

I did not know the pit he spoke of, but was glad to hear of some place, however far off, where I could lie still and get ease from the pain. And so he took me in his arms again and started off across the fields.

===Chinese===

我不知道他说的那个坑，可听说有这么一个地方——哪怕再远——能躺下来歇歇疼，心里总是欢喜的。于是他又把我抱起来，动身穿过田野去了。

===Original===

I need not tell of that weary journey, and indeed could not, if I wished; for the pain went to my head and filled me with such a drowsy anguish that I knew nothing except when some unlooked-for movement gave me a sharper twinge, and made me cry out. At first Elzevir walked briskly, but as the day wore on went slower, and was fain more than once to put me down and rest, till at last he could only carry me a hundred yards at a time. It was after noon, for the sun was past the meridian, and very hot for the time of year, when the face of the country began to change; and instead of the short sward of the open down, sprinkled with tiny white snail-shells, the ground was brashy with flat stones, and divided up into tillage fields. It was a bleak wide-bitten place enough, looking as if ’twould never pay for turning, and instead of hedges there were dreary walls built of dry stone without mortar. Behind one of these walls, broken down in places, but held together with straggling ivy, and buttressed here and there with a bramble-bush, Elzevir put me down at length and said, “I am beat, and can carry thee no farther for this present, though there is not now much farther to go. We have passed Purbeck Gates, and these walls will screen us from prying eyes if any chance comer pass along the down. And as for the soldiers, they are not like to come this way so soon, and if they come I cannot help it; for weariness and the sun’s heat have made my feet like lead. A score of years ago I would have laughed at such a task, but now ’tis different, and I must take a little sleep and rest till the air is cooler. So sit thee here and lean thy shoulder up against the wall, and thus thou canst look through this broken place and watch both ways. Then, if thou see aught moving, wake me up.﻿—I wish I had a thimbleful of powder to make this whistle sound”﻿—and he took Maskew’s silver-butted pistol again from his bosom, and handled it lovingly﻿—“ ’tis like my evil luck to carry firearms thirty years, and leave them at home at a pinch like this.” With that he flung himself down where there was a narrow shadow close against the bottom of the wall, and in a minute I knew from his heavy breathing that he was asleep.

===Chinese===

那段疲乏的旅程用不着我细说，其实想说也说不出；因为疼痛直往我脑袋里钻，灌了我一脑子昏沉沉的苦楚，我一直迷迷糊糊，只有哪个想不到的动作绞出更狠的一阵疼时，才疼醒过来叫出声。起先 Elzevir 走得还快，可天色越走越晚，他越走越慢，不止一次只得把我放下歇脚，到末了，他一次至多背得动一百码。那时已过正午——太阳过了中天，在这个季节里热得很——地面的样子开始变了；开阔坡地上那短短的草皮不见了，草皮上原撒着细小的白蜗壳，如今地里满是碎平的石片，划成一块块耕地。那是一片荒凉、被啃得七零八落的地方，瞧那样子，翻土也翻不出什么名堂来；围界不用树篱，却是些干石垒成、不抹灰浆的凄凉石墙。Elzevir 终于把我放在一道这样的墙后头——那墙有几处已经塌了，靠攀爬的常春藤连缀着，这里那里又有荆棘丛替它撑着腰——说道：“我垮了，眼下再也背不动你了，好在剩下的路也不多。咱们已过了珀贝克隘口（Purbeck Gates），有谁偶然打坡上过路，这些墙也能挡住窥探的眼睛。至于兵，一时不至于往这边来；真来了我也没办法，乏劲和暑热已经把我的脚变成铅了。二十年前，这点营生我只当笑话看；如今不同了，我得趁凉快些之前，睡一小觉歇一歇。你就坐在这儿，肩膀靠着墙，从这个豁口两边都望得见。看见有什么东西动，就叫醒我。——可惜我没有一撮火药，好叫这玩意儿出个声响”——他又从怀里掏出 Maskew 那把银柄手枪来，爱惜地摩挲着——“我背了三十年枪，偏偏这回急难当头，全撂在家里，这就是我的背运。”说完他往墙根一条窄窄的阴影里一躺，不消一分钟，我听他那沉沉的呼吸，知道他已睡着了。

===Original===

The wind had freshened much, and was blowing strong from the west; and now that I was under the lee of the wall I began to perceive that drowsiness creeping upon me which overtakes a man who has been tousled for an hour or two by the wind, and gets at length into shelter. Moreover, though I was not tired by grievous toil like Elzevir, I had passed a night without sleep, and felt besides the weariness of pain to lull me to slumber. So it was, that before a quarter of an hour was past, I had much ado to keep awake, for all I knew that I was left on guard. Then I sought something to fix my thoughts, and looking on that side of the wall where the sward was, fell to counting the molehills that were cast up in numbers thereabout. And when I had exhausted them, and reckoned up thirty little heaps of dry and powdery brown earth, that lay at random on the green turf, I turned my eyes to the tillage field on the other side of the wall, and saw the inch-high blades of corn coming up between the stones. Then I fell to counting the blades, feeling glad to have discovered a reckoning that would not be exhausted at thirty, but would go on for millions, and millions, and millions; and before I had reached ten in so heroic a numeration was fast asleep.

===Chinese===

风势大增，正从西面猛吹；如今我躲在墙的背风面，便觉出那种瞌睡偷偷爬上身来——就像一个人叫风撩拨了一两个钟头、终于躲进避风处时那样。再说，我虽不像 Elzevir 那样累于苦役，却也熬了一个整夜没睡，疼痛本身也乏得人发困，哄人入梦。就这样，还不到一刻钟，我已撑着眼皮直打架，尽管明知自己是留下放哨的。我便寻些东西拴住念头，望着墙那边有草皮的一面，数起四下里一个个鼹鼠丘来。数完它们——拢共点出三十小堆干燥松散的褐土，乱撒在绿草皮上——我又把眼睛转向墙那面的耕地，看见一英寸高的麦苗正从石头缝里钻出来。我便数起麦苗来，心里还欢喜，寻着了一个数到三十也数不完的数法，能一路数下去，数上几百万、几百万、又几百万；可在这样一场豪迈的清点里还没数到十，我已睡熟了。

===Original===

A sharp noise woke me with a start that set the pain tingling in my leg, and though I could see nothing, I knew that a shot had been fired very near us. I was for waking Elzevir, but he was already full awake, and put a finger on his lip to show I should not speak. Then he crept a few paces down the wall to where an ivy bush overtopped it, enough for him to look through the leaves without being seen. He dropped down again with a look of relief, and said, “ ’Tis but a lad scaring rooks with a blunderbuss; we will not stir unless he makes this way.”

===Chinese===

一声尖响把我惊醒，震得腿里的伤一麻一麻地疼；我什么也没看见，却知道有人在我们近旁放了一枪。我正要唤醒 Elzevir，他却早已全醒了，竖起一根手指压在唇上，示意我别作声。随后他顺着墙根爬了几步，爬到一丛常春藤高过墙头的地方，刚好能从叶子缝里张望而不被人看见。他又缩回来，脸上带着宽慰，说道：“不过是个孩子，拿大口短枪轰乌鸦呢；他不往这边来，咱们就不动。”

===Original===

A minute later he said: “The boy is coming straight for the wall; we shall have to show ourselves;” and while he spoke there was a rattle of falling stones, where the boy was partly climbing and partly pulling down the dry wall, and so Elzevir stood up. The boy looked frightened, and made as if he would run off, but Elzevir passed him the time of day in a civil voice, and he stopped and gave it back.

===Chinese===

过了一分钟他说：“那孩子直冲这墙来了；咱们只好露面了。”话音未落，石头哗啦啦一阵响，那孩子半爬半扒，正扒着那道干石墙下来，Elzevir 便站起身。孩子一脸惊惶，做出要逃跑的样子，可 Elzevir 客客气气同他道了一声日安，他便站住，也回了一声。

===Original===

“What are you doing here, son?” Block asked.

===Chinese===

“你在这儿做什么，小子？”Block 问道。

===Original===

“Scaring rooks for Farmer Topp,” was the answer.

===Chinese===

“给托普（Topp）家赶乌鸦。”他答道。

===Original===

“Have you got a charge of powder to spare?” said Elzevir, showing his pistol. “I want to get a rabbit in the gorse for supper, and have dropped my flask. Maybe you’ve seen a flask in walking through the furrows?”

===Chinese===

“你可有匀得出的一膛火药？”Elzevir 说着，亮出他的手枪。“我想打只兔子当晚饭，火药筒却弄丢了。你在犁沟里走过来，兴许见过一只火药筒？”

===Original===

He whispered to me to lie still, so that it might not be perceived my leg was broken; and the boy replied:

===Chinese===

他低声嘱咐我躺着别动，免得叫人看出我的腿断了；那孩子答道：

===Original===

“No, I have seen no flask; but very like have not come the same way as you, being sent out here from Lowermoigne; and as for powder, I have little left, and must save that for the rooks, or shall get a beating for my pains.”

===Chinese===

“没有，我没见过什么火药筒；不过我很可能同你们不走一条道，我是从下莫因（Lowermoigne）打发到这儿来的。至于火药，我剩得不多了，还得留着轰乌鸦，不然要白挨一顿打。”

===Original===

“Come,” said Elzevir, “give me a charge or two, and there is half a crown for thee.” And he took the coin out of his pocket and showed it.

===Chinese===

“来，”Elzevir 说，“给我一两膛，这儿有半克朗给你。”他从口袋里掏出那枚钱，亮了亮。

===Original===

The boy’s eyes twinkled, and so would mine at so valuable a piece, and he took out from his pocket a battered cowskin flask. “Give flask and all,” said Elzevir, “and thou shalt have a crown,” and he showed him the larger coin.

===Chinese===

那孩子的眼睛亮了——这么值钱的一枚，换了我也要眼亮——他从口袋里掏出一只磨得毛糙的牛皮火药筒。“连筒子一起给，”Elzevir 说，“给你一克朗。”他又把那枚大些的钱币亮给他看。

===Original===

No time was wasted in words; Elzevir had the flask in his pocket, and the boy was biting the crown.

===Chinese===

废话一丁点儿没耽误；Elzevir 把火药筒装进了口袋，那孩子咬着那枚克朗试真伪。

===Original===

“What shot have you?” said Elzevir.

===Chinese===

“你带的什么弹？”Elzevir 问。

===Original===

“What! have you dropped your shot-flask too?” asked the boy. And his voice had something of surprise in it.

===Chinese===

“怎么！你把弹丸筒也丢了？”孩子问，话音里带着几分惊讶。

===Original===

“Nay, but my shot are over small; if thou hast a slug or two, I would take them.”

===Chinese===

“倒不是，只是我的弹丸太小；你要是有一两颗大铅弹，我倒想要。”

===Original===

“I have a dozen goose-slugs, No. 2,” said the boy; “but thou must pay a shilling for them. My master says I never am to use them, except I see a swan or buzzard, or something fit to cook, come over: I shall get a sound beating for my pains, and to be beat is worth a shilling.”

===Chinese===

“我有一打二号鹅弹，”孩子说，“可你得出一先令买。我们东家说了，除非望见天鹅或者鹞鹰飞过，或是有什么下得锅的东西，绝不许我用它：我这一回非结结实实挨顿打不可，挨一顿打，值一先令。”

===Original===

“If thou art beat, be beat for something more,” says Elzevir the tempter. “Give me that firelock that thou carriest, and take a guinea.”

===Chinese===

“既然要挨打，就不如为个大些的由头挨，”诱人的 Elzevir 说，“把你背的那杆枪给我，收一几尼。”

===Original===

“Nay, I know not,” says the boy; “there are queer tales afloat at Lowermoigne, how that a Posse met the Contraband this morning, and shots were fired, and a gauger got an overdose of lead﻿—maybe of goose slugs No. 2. The smugglers got off clear, but they say the hue and cry is up already, and that a head-price will be fixed of twenty pound. So if I sell you a fowling-piece, maybe I shall do wrong, and have the government upon me as well as my master.” The surprise in his voice was changed to suspicion, for while he spoke I saw that his eye had fallen on my foot, though I tried to keep it in the shadow; and that he saw the boot clotted with blood, and the kerchief tied round my leg.

===Chinese===

“嘿，那可不知道，”孩子说，“下莫因那边正有些古怪传闻呢，说今儿早晨缉私队撞上了私货帮，开了枪，一个税吏吃多了铅——兴许就是二号鹅弹。走私的倒脱身走了，可都说海捕文书已经发出去了，人头价要定到二十镑。这么着，我要是卖你一杆猎枪，只怕就造了孽，东家之外，连官府也要寻到头上。”他话音里的惊讶已换成了疑心；因为他说这话的当儿，我看见他的眼睛落到了我的脚上——我原想把它藏在阴影里——他瞧见了那只结满血痂的靴子，也瞧见了我腿上扎着的手巾。

===Original===

“ ’Tis for that very reason,” says Elzevir, “that I want the firelock. These smugglers are roaming loose, and a pistol is a poor thing to stop such wicked rascals on a lone hillside. Come, come, *thou* dost not want a piece to guard thee; they will not hurt a boy.”

===Chinese===

“正是为了这个，”Elzevir 说，“我才要那杆枪。这些走私贩正四下流窜，荒山坡上，想凭一杆手枪挡住这班恶棍，可太寒碜了。来来，你一个小孩子，要枪护身做什么；他们不会伤害孩子。”

===Original===

He had the guinea between his finger and thumb, and the gleam of the gold was too strong to be withstood. So we gained a sorry matchlock, slugs, and powder, and the boy walked off over the furrow, whistling with his hand in his pocket, and a guinea and a crown-piece in his hand.

===Chinese===

他把那枚几尼捻在拇指和食指之间，金子的光太晃眼，谁也抵挡不住。就这样，我们得了一杆寒碜的火绳枪、铅弹和火药；那孩子一手插在口袋里，一手攥着一枚几尼一枚克朗，吹着口哨，踩着犁沟走了。

===Original===

His whistle sounded innocent enough, yet I mistrusted him, having caught his eye when he was looking at my bloody foot; and so I said as much to Elzevir, who only laughed, saying the boy was simple and harmless. But from where I sat I could peep out through the brambles in the open gap, and see without being seen﻿—and there was my young gentleman walking carelessly enough, and whistling like any bird so long as Elzevir’s head was above the wall; but when Elzevir sat down, the boy gave a careful look round, and seeing no one watching any more, dropped his whistling and made off as fast as heels would carry him. Then I knew that he had guessed who we were, and was off to warn the hue and cry; but before Elzevir was on his feet again, the boy was out of sight, over the hill-brow.

===Chinese===

他那口哨听上去再无辜不过，我却信不过他，因为我撞见过他打量我血脚的那道眼神；我便把这层意思对 Elzevir 说了，他只笑，说那孩子又老实又无妨。可从我坐的地方，能从豁口里的荆棘缝里偷看出去，看得见人，人不看见我——只见我这位小爷走得何等散漫，只要 Elzevir 的头还在墙顶上头，他就一路像只鸟那样吹着口哨；待 Elzevir 一坐下，那孩子便小心四下一张望，见再没有人看他了，口哨一撂，撒开脚丫子飞快跑了。这下我知道他已经猜出我们是谁，报信去了；可不等 Elzevir 再站起来，那孩子已经翻过山坡，没了影儿。

===Original===

“Let us move on,” said Block; “ ’tis but a little distance now to go, and the heat is past already. We must have slept three hours or more, for thou art but a sorry watchman, John. ’Tis when the sentry sleeps that the enemy laughs, and for thee the Posse might have had us both like daylight owls.”

===Chinese===

“咱们走吧，”Block 说，“如今没多少路了，暑气也过去了。咱们准是睡了三个钟头还不止，你这块料，当真是个蹩脚的哨兵，John。哨兵一睡，敌人就该笑啦；多亏了你，差一点儿缉私队就把咱们俩像白昼里的猫头鹰似的，一窝端了。”

===Original===

With that he took me on his back and made off with a lusty stride, keeping as much as possible under the brow of the hill and in the shelter of the walls. We had slept longer than we thought, for the sun was westering fast, and though the rest had refreshed me, my leg had grown stiff, and hurt the more in dangling when we started again. Elzevir was still walking strongly, in spite of the heavy burden he carried, and in less than half an hour I knew, though I had never been there before, we were in the land of the old marble quarries at the back of Anvil Point.

===Chinese===

说罢他把我背上背，迈开健壮的大步走了，尽量贴着山脊的背阴、躲在石墙的遮蔽里走。我们睡的工夫比想的还长，太阳已经飞快地偏西了；歇息虽叫我缓过了劲，腿却僵了，重新上路一悬荡，疼得反而更凶。Elzevir 尽管驮着这么一副重担，步子仍旧强劲；不到半个钟头，我虽从没到过那地方，也知道我们已经走进了铁砧角（Anvil Point）背后那片古老大理石采石场的地界了。

===Original===

Although I knew little of these quarries, and certainly was in evil plight to take note of anything at that time, yet afterwards I learnt much about them. Out of such excavations comes that black Purbeck Marble which you see in old churches in our country, and I am told in other parts of England as well. And the way of making a marble quarry is to sink a tunnel, slanting very steeply down into the earth, like a well turned askew, till you reach fifty, seventy, or perhaps one hundred feet deep. Then from the bottom of this shaft there spread out narrow passages or tunnels, mostly six feet high, but sometimes only three or four, and in these the marble is dug. These quarries were made by men centuries ago, some say by the Romans themselves; and though some are still worked in other parts of Purbeck, those at the back of Anvil Point have been disused beyond the memory of man.

===Chinese===

这些采石场我当时知道得很少，何况那时节我的处境也坏得顾不上留意什么；倒是后来我对它们知道了许多。我们这一带老教堂里见的那种黑色珀贝克大理石，据说英格兰别处也有，就是从这样的矿坑里出来的。开大理石矿的法子，是往地里凿一条斜井，斜得极陡地插下去，像一口扭歪了的井，一直凿到五十英尺、七十英尺、或许一百英尺深处。然后从井底往四外伸展出一道道窄巷，或者说隧道，多半六英尺高，有时只有三四英尺，大理石就在这些巷道里开采。这些矿坑是几百年前的人开下的，有人说就是罗马人自己开的；珀贝克别处的一些矿坑如今还在开采，铁砧角背后这些，却荒废得没人记得起了。

===Original===

We had left the stony village fields, and the face of the country was covered once more with the closest sward, which was just putting on the brighter green of spring. This turf was not smooth, but hummocky, for under it lay heaps of worthless stone and marble drawn out of the quarries ages ago, which the green vestment had covered for the most part, though it left sometimes a little patch of broken rubble peering out at the top of a mound. There were many tumbledown walls and low gables left of the cottages of the old quarrymen; grass-covered ridges marked out the little garden-folds, and here and there still stood a forlorn gooseberry-bush, or a stunted plum- or apple-tree with its branches all swept eastward by the up-Channel gales. As for the quarry shafts themselves, they too were covered round the tips with the green turf, and down them led a narrow flight of steep-cut steps, with a slide of soapstone at the side, on which the marble blocks were once hauled up by wooden winches. Down these steps no feet ever walked now, for not only were suffocating gases said to beset the bottom of the shafts, but men would have it that in the narrow passages below lurked evil spirits and demons. One who ought to know about such things, told me that when St. Aldhelm first came to Purbeck, he bound the old pagan gods under a ban deep in these passages, but that the worst of all the crew was a certain demon called the Mandrive, who watched over the best of the black marble. And that was why such marble might only be used in churches or for graves, for if it were not for this holy purpose, the Mandrive would have power to strangle the man that hewed it.

===Chinese===

我们已把村里那些多石的田地抛在身后，地面又盖满了细密的草皮，正披上春天更亮的绿。这草皮并不平整，而是疙疙瘩瘩的，因为底下堆着多少年前从矿坑里拖出来的一堆堆废石和废大理石，绿色的罩衫把它们大半都罩住了，只是有时在一个土墩顶上，还探出一小片破碎的碎石。老采石工们的村舍，留下许多坍塌的墙和低矮的山墙；长满草的垄埂勾出一方方小小的园圃，这里那里还立着一丛孤零零的醋栗，或是一株矮瘦的李树、苹果树，枝条全叫顺着海峡刮上来的风暴扫得倒向东边。至于那些矿井本身，井口四围也叫绿草皮盖着，井里下去是一溜窄而陡的凿阶，旁边带着一道皂石滑槽，从前大理石块就是用木绞车顺着它绞上来的。如今再没有一只脚走这些石阶了，因为据说井底尽是憋死人的瘴气，人们还一口咬定，底下那些窄巷里潜着妖邪和魔鬼。一位照理深谙此道的人告诉我：圣奥尔德赫姆（St. Aldhelm）初到珀贝克的时候，把那些古老的异教神明下了一道禁咒，捆在这些巷子的深处；可那一伙里最歹毒的，是一个叫曼德赖夫（the Mandrive）的魔头，看守着最好的黑大理石。所以那种大理石只许用在教堂里或做墓碑，因为若不是为了这神圣的用途，曼德赖夫就有本事掐死采石的人。

===Original===

It was by the side of one of these old shafts that Elzevir laid me down at last. The light was very low, showing all the little unevennesses of the turf; and the sward crept over the edges of the hole, and every crack and crevice in steps and slide was green with ferns. The green ferns shrouded the walls of the hole, and ruddy brown brambles overgrew the steps, till all was lost in the gloom that hung at the bottom of the pit.

===Chinese===

Elzevir 终于把我放在一口这样的老井旁边。光线已极低了，照出草皮上一切细小的起伏；草皮爬过洞口的边缘，石阶和滑槽上每一道裂缝里都绿满了蕨草。绿蕨遮住了洞壁，红褐色的荆棘漫过了石阶，再往下，一切都没入井底悬着的那片幽暗里。

===Original===

Elzevir drew a deep breath or two of the cool evening air, like a man who has come through a difficult trial.

===Chinese===

Elzevir 吸了一两口清凉的晚风，像一个闯过了一场艰难考验的人。

===Original===

“There,” he said, “this is Joseph’s Pit, and here we must lie hid until thy foot is sound again. Once get to the bottom safe, and we can laugh at posse, and hue and cry, and at the King’s Crown itself. They cannot search all the quarries, and are not like to search any of them, for they are cowards at the best, and hang much on tales of the Mandrive. Ay, and such tales are true enough, for there lurk gases at the bottom of most of the shafts, like devils to strangle any that go down. And if they do come down this Joseph’s Pit, we still have nineteen chances in a score they cannot thread the workings. But last, if they come down, and thread the path, there is this pistol and a rusty matchlock; and before they come to where we lie, we can hold the troop at bay and sell our lives so dear they will not care to buy them.”

===Chinese===

“到了，”他说，“这就是约瑟夫坑；咱们要在这里藏到你那只脚好利索为止。只要平安下到井底，缉私队也好，海捕文书也好，连国王的王冠本身也好，咱们都可以笑一笑。他们搜不完这些矿坑，多半连一个也不搜，因为这班人充其量是些懦夫，又极信曼德赖夫那些传说。啊，那些传说也真不假，多数井底都潜伏着瘴气，像魔鬼一样，专憋死下去的人。他们果真下这个约瑟夫坑来，咱们也还有二十分里的十九分把握，他们穿不过这些矿道。退一万步，他们下来了，又穿过了矿道，还有这杆手枪和一杆生锈的火绳枪；不等他们到咱们躺的地方，咱们就能顶住这一队人，把性命卖个天价，叫他们出不起这个价。”

===Original===

We waited a few minutes, and then he took me in his arms and began to descend the steps, back first, as one goes down a hatchway. The sun was setting in a heavy bank of clouds just as we began to go down, and I could not help remembering how I had seen it set over peaceful Moonfleet only twenty-four hours ago; and how far off we were now, and how long it was likely to be before I saw that dear village and Grace again.

===Chinese===

我们等了几分钟，然后他把我抱进怀里，背朝前顺石阶往下退，像人下舱口那样。我们刚要下井，太阳正沉进一大堆云里；我不由想起，不过二十四小时以前，我还见它落在宁静的 Moonfleet 上空；如今我们离那里多么远了，离我再见到那可爱的村庄、再见到 Grace，又该多么久啊。

===Original===

The stairs were still sharp cut and little worn, but Elzevir paid great care to his feet, lest he should slip on the ferns and mosses with which they were overgrown. When we reached the brambles he met them with his back, and though I heard the thorns tearing in his coat, he shoved them aside with his broad shoulders, and screened my dangling leg from getting caught. Thus he came safe without stumble to the bottom of the pit.

===Chinese===

石阶还棱角崭新，磨损得少，可 Elzevir 对脚下丝毫不敢大意，生怕踩着满阶的蕨草和苔藓滑下去。走到荆棘丛那里，他用背去迎它们；我听得见棘刺撕扯他外衣的声音，他却用宽阔的肩膀把它们拱开，护住我悬荡的腿，不让它被勾住。他就这样一步不失、安安稳稳地到了井底。

===Original===

When we got there all was dark, but he stepped off into a narrow opening on the right hand, and walked on as if he knew the way. I could see nothing, but perceived that we were passing through endless galleries cut in the solid rock, high enough, for the most part, to allow of walking upright, but sometimes so low as to force him to bend down and carry me in a very constrained attitude. Only twice did he set me down at a turning, while he took out his tinderbox and lit a match; but at length the darkness became less dark, and I saw that we were in a large cave or room, into which the light came through some opening at the far end. At the same time I felt a colder breath and fresh salt smell in the air that told me we were very near the sea.

===Chinese===

到了井底，四下里一片黑，他却向右手一道窄口跨过去，往前走，仿佛认得路似的。我什么也看不见，只觉出我们正穿过一条条凿在实岩里的无尽长廊，多半高得能直着身子走，有时又低得逼他弯下腰，用一种极别扭的姿势抱着我。只有两次，他在拐角处把我放下，取出火绒盒，点着一根火媒；可到末了，黑渐渐不那么黑了，我看出我们进了一个大洞，或者说一间大屋，光从远处那头一个什么口子里透进来。同时我觉出空气里有一股更冷的气息和新鲜的咸味，告诉我大海已经近在咫尺了。

## XI The Sea-Cave / 十一 海边岩洞

===Original===

“The dull loneness, the black shade,
						

						That these hanging vaults have made;
						

						The strange music of the waves
						

						Beating on these hollow caves.”

> Wither

===Chinese===

这沉闷的孤寂、这黑黢黢的幽暗，
都出自这高悬的穹顶；
海浪拍打着空洞的岩窟，
奏出这一片奇异的乐声。

> Wither

===Original===

He set me down in one corner, where was some loose dry silver-sand upon the floor, which others had perhaps used for a resting-place before. “Thou must lie here for a month or two, lad,” he said; “ ’tis a mean bed, but I have known many worse, and will get straw tomorrow if I can, to better it.”

===Chinese===

他把我安放在一个角落里，那儿的地上有一摊松散干爽的银白沙，兴许早先也有人拿它当过歇脚的地方。“你得在这儿躺上一两个月，孩子，”他说，“这张床是寒碜了点儿，可我见过比它糟得多的；明天要是办得成，我去弄些稻草来，好叫它强些。”

===Original===

I had eaten nothing all day, nor had Elzevir, yet I felt no hunger, only a giddiness and burning thirst like that which came upon me when I was shut in the Mohune vault. So ’twas very music to me to hear a pat and splash of water dropping from the roof into a little pool upon the floor, and Elzevir made a cup out of my hat and gave a full drink of it that was icy-cool and more delicious than any smuggled wine of France.

===Chinese===

我一整天没吃一点东西，Elzevir 也一样，可我并不觉得饿，只觉得头晕，喉咙火烧火燎地渴，同我被关在 Mohune 家墓窖里那回发作的一模一样。所以，听见水珠从洞顶滴进地上一小汪水潭、啪嗒啪嗒地溅响，在我听来简直如闻乐音；Elzevir 拿我的帽子做个杯儿，舀了满满一杯给我喝。那水冰凉沁骨，比法兰西任何走私来的美酒都更甘美。

===Original===

And after that I knew little that happened for ten days or more, for fever had hold of me, and as I learnt afterwards, I talked wild and could scarce be restrained from jumping up and loosing the bindings that Elzevir had put upon my leg. And all that time he nursed me as tenderly as any mother could her child, and never left the cave except when he was forced to seek food. But after the fever passed it left me very thin, as I could see from hands and arms, and weaker than a baby; and I used to lie the whole day, not thinking much, nor troubling about anything, but eating what was given me and drawing a quiet pleasure from the knowledge that strength was gradually returning. Elzevir had found a battered sea-chest up on Peveril Point, and from the side of it made splints to set my leg﻿—using his own shirt for bandages. The sand-bed too was made more soft and easy with some armfuls of straw, and in one corner of the cave was a little pile of driftwood and an iron cooking-pot. And all these things had Elzevir got by foraging of nights, using great care that none should see him, and taking only what would not be much missed or thought about; but soon he contrived to give Ratsey word of where we were, and after that the sexton fended for us. There were none even of the landers knew what was become of us, save only Ratsey; and he never came down the quarry, but would leave what he brought in one of the ruined cottages a half-mile from the shaft. And all the while there was strict search being made for us, and mounted Excisemen scouring the country; for though at first the Posse took back Maskew’s dead body and said we must have fallen over the cliff, for there was nothing to be found of us, yet afterwards a farm-boy brought a tale of how he had come suddenly on men lurking under a wall, and how one had a bloody foot and leg, and how the other sprung upon him and after a fierce struggle wrenched his master’s rook-piece from his hands, rifled his pocket of a powder-horn, and made off with them like a hare towards Corfe. And as to Maskew, some of the soldiers said that Elzevir had shot him, and others that he died by misadventure, being killed by a stray bullet of one of his own men on the hilltop; but for all that they put a head-price on Elzevir of 50, and 20 for me, so we had reason to lie close. It must have been Maskew that listened that night at the door when Elzevir told me the hour at which the cargo was to be run; for the Posse had been ordered to be at Hoar Head at four in the morning. So all the gang would have been taken had it not been for the *Gulder* making earlier, and the soldiers being delayed by tippling at the Lobster.

===Chinese===

那以后有十来天，我几乎不知道身边出过什么事，因为热病缠住了我；据我后来得知，我满口呓语，几番要跳起来、去解 Elzevir 绑在我腿上的绷带，好不容易才按捺得住。在那整段日子里，他看护我体贴得就像最慈爱的母亲待亲生孩子，除了不得不出去寻吃食，从不离开岩洞。热病退了，却把我熬得极瘦——看看自己的手和胳膊便知道——而且比婴孩还弱；我便整天躺着，不大思想，也不为什么操心，给什么吃什么，从力气正一天天回来这桩事里，静静咂摸出一股欢喜。Elzevir 在佩弗里尔角（Peveril Point）上寻着一口破旧的航海箱，从箱板上劈出夹板来给我接腿——绷带用的他自己的衬衫。沙铺也添了几抱稻草，愈发松软受用；洞的一角堆着一小垛浮柴和一只铁炊锅。这些东西都是 Elzevir 夜里出外觅来的，处处当心不叫人瞧见，只拿那些不大会被人记挂、惹人疑心的东西；可不久他还是设法给 Ratsey 递了信儿，告诉他我们的下落；打那以后，这位教堂司事便替我们张罗起来。就连跑私货的那伙人里，也没人知道我们遭了什么，唯独 Ratsey 例外；他也从不亲自下到采石场来，只把带来的东西撂在离井口半英里的一座破败小屋里。而这段时间里，官府一直在严严实实地搜捕我们，骑马的税吏把乡野梳了一遍又一遍；因为起先缉私队虽抬回了 Maskew 的尸首，断言我们多半坠了崖——我们两个踪影全无——可后来有个庄户孩子带出一段话来，说他怎样冷不防撞见两个人伏在一堵墙底下，一个怎样满脚满腿是血，另一个怎样朝他扑上来，经过一场死命的挣扎，把他主人那杆打白嘴鸦的枪从他手里夺了去，又从他口袋里搜走一只火药角，然后带着这两样东西，野兔似的朝科夫（Corfe）一溜烟跑了。至于 Maskew，士兵们有的说是 Elzevir 开枪打死的，有的说他死于不测，叫自己人在冈顶上的一颗流弹送了命；可尽管这样，官府还是给 Elzevir 的脑袋悬了五十镑的赏，给我的悬了二十镑，所以我们大有理由趴着不动。那天夜里 Elzevir 告诉我卸货时辰的时候，在门外偷听的准是 Maskew；因为缉私队奉到的命令，就是清晨四点赶到 Hoar Head。倘若不是“Gulder”潮提早来了，士兵们又耽搁在“Lobster”客栈里贪杯灌酒，这一伙人本是要一网打尽的。

===Original===

All this Elzevir learnt from Ratsey and told me to pass the time, though in truth I had as lief not heard it, for ’tis no pleasant thing to see one’s head wrote down so low as 20. And what I wanted most to know, namely how Grace fared and how she took the bad news of her father’s death, I could not hear, for Elzevir said nothing, and I was shy to ask him.

===Chinese===

这些事都是 Elzevir 从 Ratsey 那里听来、讲给我解闷的；其实我宁可没听过，因为眼看着自己的脑袋作价低到二十镑，终究不是桩愉快的事。而我顶顶想知道的——Grace 过得怎样，她怎样承受了父亲死讯的噩耗——却无从听见，因为 Elzevir 只字不提，我又羞于启齿问他。

===Original===

Now when I came entirely to myself, and was able to take stock of things, I found that the place in which I lay was a cave some eight yards square and three in height, whose straight-cut walls showed that men had once hewed stone therefrom. On one side was that passage through which we had come in, and on the other opened a sort of door which gave on to a stone ledge eight fathoms above high-water mark. For the cave was cut out just inside that iron cliff-face which lies between St. Alban’s Head and Swanage. But the cliffs here are different from those on the other side of the Head, being neither so high as Hoar Head nor of chalk, but standing for the most part only an hundred or an hundred and fifty feet above the sea, and showing towards it a stern face of solid rock. But though they rise not so high above the water, they go down a long way below it; so that there is fifty fathom right up to the cliff, and many a good craft out of reckoning in fog, or on a pitch-dark night, has run full against that frowning wall, and perished, ship and crew, without a soul to hear their cries. Yet, though the rock looks hard as adamant, the eternal washing of the wave has worn it out below, and even with the slightest swell there is a dull and distant booming of the surge in those cavernous deeps; and when the wind blows fresh, each roller smites the cliff like a thunderclap, till even the living rock trembles again.

===Chinese===

等我完全清醒过来、能细细打量四周的时候，才发现自己躺的地方是个岩洞，约摸八码见方、三码来高，四壁切得笔直，可见从前有人在里头采凿过石头。一边是我们进来的那条通道，另一边开着一个门样的口子，通到高出满潮线八英寻的一道石台上。因为这洞正凿在 St. Alban's Head 与斯沃尼奇（Swanage）之间那面铁色崖壁的内侧。这一带的崖又同海角那边的不同，既没有 Hoar Head 那样高，也不是白垩的，大半只拔海一百或一百五十英尺，朝海露着一张坚硬岩石的冷峻面孔。不过它们虽然出水不高，水底下却扎下去老深；所以紧贴崖脚就有五十英寻的水，多少好船在大雾里、在漆黑夜里失了算计，一头撞上那堵森然的高墙，船和人一齐送命，喊哑了嗓子也没有一个魂灵听见。然而岩石虽看着坚硬如金刚，海浪亘古的淘刷早把底下掏空了；哪怕只起最轻的涌，那些洞窟深处也会应着一阵低沉遥远的轰响；风一起劲，每一个巨浪砸在崖上便如一声炸雷，连活的岩石都跟着震颤。

===Original===

It was on a ledge of that rock-face that our cave opened, and sometimes on a fine day Elzevir would carry me out thither, so that I might sun myself and see all the moving Channel without myself being seen. For this ledge was carved out something like a balcony, so that when the quarry was in working they could lower the stone by pulleys to boats lying underneath, and perhaps haul up a keg or two by the way of ballast, as might be guessed by the stanchions still rusting in the rock.

===Chinese===

我们的洞口，正开在那面崖壁的一道石台上；赶上好天，Elzevir 有时便把我抱到外头去，让我晒晒太阳，望遍整条流动的海峡，自己却不被人瞧见。原来这道石台凿得有些像一座露台，从前采石场开着工的时候，人们好用滑轮把石头吊到底下候着的船上，兴许还顺路吊上去一两只桶权充压舱——岩缝里锈到如今的那些立柱，便可作证。

===Original===

Such was this gallery; and as for the inside of the cave, ’twas a great empty room, with a white floor made up of broken stone-dust trodden hard of old till one would say it was plaster; and dry, without those sweaty damps so often seen in such places﻿—save only in one corner a land-spring dropped from the roof trickling down over spiky rock-icicles, and falling into a little hollow in the floor. This basin had been scooped out of set purpose, with a gutter seaward for the overflow, and round it and on the wet patch of the roof above grew a garden of ferns and other clinging plants.

===Chinese===

这道石廊便是如此；至于洞的里头，则是一间空旷的大屋，白地面由碎石屑旧年踩成，踩得硬硬实实，简直像抹了层灰泥；又干燥，没有这类去处常有的那种黏汗的潮气——只除开一个角落：一股伏泉从洞顶滴下，顺着尖耸如冰凌的岩石涓涓流坠，落进地面一个小小的洼坑。这盆坑是特意凿出来的，还朝海开了一道水沟，好排泄溢流；坑的四周和顶上那片湿漉漉的石面上，长着一园蕨草和别的攀石植物。

===Original===

The weeks moved on until we were in the middle of May, when even the nights were no longer cold, as the sun gathered power. And with the warmer days my strength too increased, and though I dared not yet stand, my leg had ceased to pain me, except for some sharp twinges now and then, which Elzevir said were caused by the bone setting. And then he would put a poultice made of grass upon the place, and once walked almost as far as Chaldron to pluck sorrel for a soothing mash.

===Chinese===

一星期一星期过去，转眼到了五月中旬，太阳一日盛似一日，夜里也不再冷了。随着天气转暖，我的力气也长了上来；虽然还不敢站，腿倒不疼了，只时不时抽痛几下，Elzevir 说那是接骨生拢的缘故。碰到这时，他就拿青草捣成一帖糊敷在伤处，有一回还几乎走到查尔德伦（Chaldron），去采酸模给我做一服镇痛的药糊。

===Original===

Now though he had gone out and in so many times in safety, yet I was always ill at ease when he was away, lest he might fall into some ambush and never come back. Nor was it any thought of what would come to me if he were caught that grieved me, but only care for him; for I had come to lean in everything upon this grim and grizzled giant, and love him like a father. So when he was away I took to reading to beguile my thoughts; but found little choice of matter, having only my aunt’s red Prayerbook that I thrust into my bosom the afternoon that I left Moonfleet, and Blackbeard’s locket. For that locket hung always round my neck; and I often had the parchment out and read it; not that I did not know it now by heart, but because reading it seemed to bring Grace to my thoughts, for the last time I had read it was when I saw her in the Manor Woods.

===Chinese===

他虽平平安安出出进进过这么多遭，可只要他一离开，我心里总不踏实，生怕他中了什么埋伏，一去不回。叫我难过的，倒不是想着他若被拿住、我会落个什么下场，而只是牵挂他本人；因为我已事事倚仗这个面色冷峻、须发斑白的巨人，像爱父亲一样爱他。所以他不在跟前，我便靠读书排遣心思；可没什么可挑的，手头只有姑妈那本红皮《公祷书》——离开 Moonfleet 那天下午，我把它揣进了怀里——还有 Blackbeard 的坠盒。那坠盒终日挂在我脖子上；我常把羊皮纸抽出来读；不是到这时还没背熟，而是读着它，Grace 仿佛就上了我的心头，因为我末一遭读它，正是在庄园林子里见她的时候。

===Original===

Elzevir and I had often talked over what was to be done when my leg should be sound again, and resolved to take passage to St. Malo in the *Bonaventure*, and there lie hid till the pursuit against us should have ceased. For though ’twas wartime, French and English were as brothers in the contraband, and the shippers would give us bit and sup, and glad to, as long as we had need of them. But of this I need not say more, because ’twas but a project, which other events came in to overturn.

===Chinese===

Elzevir 和我常商量，等我的腿好利索了该当如何，末了定下搭乘“Bonaventure”号去圣马洛，在那边藏身，等追捕我们的风声平息。虽说两国正打着仗，法兰西人和英格兰人在私货行当里却亲如兄弟；只要我们还用得着他们，船家们自会供我们一口饭吃，而且供得心甘情愿。这个我也不必多讲，因为那不过是个打算，后来叫别的事变推翻了。

===Original===

Yet ’twas this very errand, namely, to fix with the *Bonaventure*’s men the time to take us over to the other side, that Elzevir had gone out, on the day of which I shall now speak. He was to go to Poole, and left our cave in the afternoon, thinking it safe to keep along the cliff-edge even in the daylight, and to strike across country when dusk came on. The wind had blown fresh all the morning from southwest, and after Elzevir had left, strengthened to a gale. My leg was now so strong that I could walk across the cave with the help of a stout blackthorn that Elzevir had cut me: and so I went out that afternoon on to the ledge to watch the growing sea. There I sat down, with my back against a protecting rock, in such a place that I could see up-Channel and yet shelter from the rushing wind. The sky was overcast, and the long wall of rock showed grey with orange-brown patches and a darker line of seaweed at the base like the under strake of a boat’s belly, for the tide was but beginning to make. There was a mist, half-fog, half-spray, scudding before the wind, and through it I could see the white-backed rollers lifting over Peveril Point; while all along the cliff-face the seabirds thronged the ledges, and sat huddled in snowy lines, knowing the mischief that was brewing in the elements.

===Chinese===

然而我现在要讲的这一天，Elzevir 出门办的恰是这桩差事：同“Bonaventure”号上的人敲定载我们去对岸的日子。他要去普尔，下午便离了岩洞，想着大白天沿着崖沿走也未尝不稳妥，等暮色四合再斜穿乡野过去。一早晨风都从西南方刮得紧，Elzevir 走后，更越刮越凶，成了风暴。我这条腿如今已经硬朗，拄着 Elzevir 给我砍的那根结实的黑刺李木杖，能横穿过岩洞了：于是那天下午，我便出到石台上，看那越涨越猛的大海。我在那里坐下，背靠一块挡风的岩石，坐的位置正好望得见上海峡，又避得开扑面的急风。天阴着，长长一道石壁显出灰色，间着橙棕的斑块，根脚上一道深色的海藻线，好比船肚的下缘板——那时潮才方起。半是雾半是浪沫的水汽贴着风疾飞，透过它，我望得见白脊的巨浪在佩弗里尔角外一个个隆起；而整面崖壁上，海鸟挤满了层层石台，一堆堆缩作雪白的行列——它们知道天地间正酝着一场祸事。

===Original===

It was a melancholy scene, and bred melancholy in my heart; and about sundown the wind southed a point or two, setting the sea more against the cliff, so that the spray began to fly even over my ledge and drove me back into the cave. The night came on much sooner than usual, and before long I was lying on my straw bed in perfect darkness. The wind had gone still more to south, and was screaming through the opening of the cave; the caverns down below bellowed and rumbled; every now and then a giant roller struck the rock such a blow as made the cave tremble, and then a second later there would fall, splattering on the ledge outside, the heavy spray that had been lifted by the impact.

===Chinese===

这是一幅凄凉的景象，也在我心里酿出凄凉；约莫日落时分，风向又南转了一两个点，海越发顶着崖打，浪花开始连我的石台都飞溅了过来，把我赶回洞里。夜来得比往常早得多，不多时我已躺在稻草铺上，四下里一片漆黑。风更往南转，在洞口尖声呼啸；底下的洞窟轰轰隆隆地怒吼；不时一个巨浪砸在岩石上，那一记直震得岩洞发颤，跟着一秒之后，被这一撞掀起的重重浪花，便噼噼啪啪泼洒在外面的石台上。

===Original===

I have said that I was melancholy; but worse followed, for I grew timid, and fearful of the wild night, and the loneliness, and the darkness. And all sorts of evil tales came to my mind, and I thought much of baleful heathen gods that St. Aldhelm had banished to these underground cellars, and of the Mandrive who leapt on people in the dark and strangled them. And then fancy played another trick on me, and I seemed to see a man lying on the cave-floor with a drawn white face upturned, and a red hole in the forehead; and at last could bear the dark no longer, but got up with my lame leg and groped round till I found a candle, for we had two or three in store. ’Twas only with much ado I got it lit and set up in the corner of the cave, and then I sat down close by trying to screen it with my coat. But do what I would the wind came gusting round the corner, blowing the flame to one side, and making the candle gutter as another candle guttered on that black day at the Why Not? And so thought whisked round till I saw Maskew’s face wearing a look of evil triumph, when the pin fell at the auction, and again his face grew deadly pale, and there was the bullet-mark on his brow.

===Chinese===

我已说过，我心里凄凉；可更糟的还在后头：我变得胆怯了，怕这狂暴的夜、这孤寂、这黑暗。种种邪恶的故事都涌上心头，我一再想起圣奥尔德赫姆贬到这些地下窖穴里的凶神，想起那个黑暗里跳到人身上、扼死人喉咙的曼德赖夫。跟着幻想又跟我耍了另一个花头，我仿佛看见一个人躺在洞底地上，仰着一张扭曲的白脸，额头上一个红窟窿；到末了我实在受不住这黑暗了，便拖着瘸腿爬起来，四处摸索，直到寻着一支蜡烛——我们原存着两三支。我费了好大的事才把它点着，安置在洞角里，然后紧挨着坐下，想拿外衣给它挡风。可随我怎么遮挡，风总是一阵一阵绕过角落灌进来，把火苗吹得歪向一边，蜡烛淌下泪来，正像“Why Not?”客栈那个黑色的日子里淌泪的另一支蜡烛。于是念头也这样打着转，转到后来，我眼前便是 Maskew 在拍卖会上针落下时那副恶毒得意的脸，跟着他的脸又变得死灰一样白，额上现出那个弹痕。

===Original===

Surely there were evil spirits in this place to lead my thoughts so much astray, and then there came to my mind that locket on my neck, which men had once hung round Blackbeard’s to scare evil spirits from his tomb. If it could frighten them from him, might it not rout them now, and make them fly from me? And with that thought I took the parchment out, and opening it before the flickering light, although I knew all, word for word, conned it over again, and read it out aloud. It was a relief to hear a human voice, even though ’twas nothing but my own, and I took to shouting the words, having much ado even so to make them heard for the raging of the storm:

===Chinese===

这地方一定藏着邪祟，才把我的念头引得这般迷乱；这时我想起脖子上那只坠盒——从前人们把它挂上 Blackbeard 的脖子，为的正是吓退他坟里的邪灵。它既吓得退他坟里的邪灵，如今未必不能把它们杀退，教它们从我眼前逃开么？想到这里，我抽出那张羊皮纸，在摇曳的烛光前展开，虽然一字一句早都烂熟，还是从头又诵读了一遍，而且读出了声。听见一个人的嗓音，哪怕那不过是我自己的，也算一桩宽慰；我便索性放声喊读起来，饶是这样，还得费尽气力，才能顶过风涛的怒号，教这些字句听得真切：

===Original===

“The days of our age are threescore years and ten; and though men be so strong that they come to fourscore years; yet is their strength then but labour and sorrow; so soon passeth it away, and we are gone.

===Chinese===

“我们一生的年日是七十岁；人纵然强壮，可到八十岁，那时所夸的也不过是劳苦愁烦，转眼之间，我们便如飞而去。

===Original===

“And as for me, my feet were almost﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“至于我，我的脚几乎……”

===Original===

At the “almost” I stopped, being brought up suddenly with a fierce beat of blood through my veins, and a jump fit to burst them, for I had heard a scuffling noise in the passage that led to the cave, as if someone had stumbled against a loose stone in the dark. I did not know then, but have learnt since, that where there is a loud noise, such as the roaring of a cascade, the churning of a mill, or, as here, the rage and bluster of a storm﻿—if there arise some different sound, even though it be as slight as the whistle of a bird, ’twill strike the ear clear above the general din. And so it was this night, for I caught that stumbling tread even when the gale blew loudest, and sat motionless and breathless, in my eagerness of listening, and then the gale lulled an instant, and I heard the slow beat of footsteps as of one groping his way down the passage in the dark. I knew it was not Elzevir, for first he could not be back from Poole for many hours yet, and second, he always whistled in a certain way to show ’twas he coming and gave besides a password; yet, if not Elzevir, who could it be? I blew out the light, for I did not want to guide the aim of some unknown marksman shooting at me from the dark; and then I thought of that gaunt strangler that sprang on marbleworkers in the gloom; yet it could not be the Mandrive, for surely he would know his own passages better than to stumble in them in the dark. It was more likely to be one of the hue and cry who had smelt us out, and hoped perhaps to be able to reconnoitre without being perceived on so awful a night. Whenever Elzevir went out foraging, he carried with him that silver-butted pistol which had once been Maskew’s, but left behind the old rook-piece. We had plenty of powder and slugs now, having obtained a store of both from Ratsey, and Elzevir had bid me keep the matchlock charged, and use it or not after my own judgement, if any came to the cave; but gave as his counsel that it was better to die fighting than to swing at Dorchester, for that we should most certainly do if taken. We had agreed, moreover, on a password, which was “Prosper the *Bonaventure*,” so that I might challenge betimes any that I heard coming, and if they gave not back this countersign might know it was not Elzevir.

===Chinese===

读到“几乎”二字，我猛地收住了：血在血管里凶猛地一涌，心口一跳，几乎要把血脉迸裂——因为我听见通向岩洞的过道里起了一阵窸窸窣窣的响动，像有人在黑暗里绊着了一块松动的石头。我那时不懂、后来才学到：凡有巨响的地方——比如瀑布的轰鸣、水磨的搅转，或者像今晚这风暴的狂怒咆哮——倘若其中冒出一丝异样的声响，哪怕轻得只像一声鸟唳，也会清清亮亮地穿透满耳的喧腾，直送进耳朵。这一晚正是这样：风刮得最响的当儿，我也捉住了那踉踉跄跄的脚步声；我坐着一动不动、大气也不敢出，凝神细听；随后风势歇了一瞬，我听见缓慢的脚步声，像一个人在黑暗里摸索着顺过道下来。我知道那不是 Elzevir：第一，他要从普尔回来，还得好几个钟头；第二，他回来时总要打一声特别的呼哨，表明来的是他，外带还对一句口令。可若不是 Elzevir，又能是谁呢？我吹灭了灯，因为我不愿给哪个从暗处朝我放枪的无名枪手指引准头；接着我又想起那个精瘦的扼杀者，专在昏黑里扑向采大理石的工人；可那决计不会是曼德赖夫，他总不至于连自家的过道都不认得，摸黑还在里头绊跤。多半是搜捕我们的那伙人里，哪一个嗅出了我们的踪迹，想趁着这样骇人的黑夜，悄悄摸进来探看而不被察觉。Elzevir 每回出门觅食，总随身带着那杆曾是 Maskew 的银柄手枪，把那杆打白嘴鸦的旧枪留在洞里。如今我们火药和铅弹都充足，是从 Ratsey 那里得了满满一批；Elzevir 吩咐我把火绳枪装好弹药，若真有人进洞来，放不放枪由我自己斟酌；不过他劝我：与其在多切斯特荡绞架，不如战斗着死——我们若被拿住，那是笃定要荡绞架的。此外我们还约定了一句口令，叫“愿‘Bonaventure’号兴旺”，这样我一听得有人前来，便可及早喝问；对方若对不上这句暗语，我就知道那不是 Elzevir。

===Original===

So now I reached out for the piece, which lay beside me on the floor, and scrambled to my feet; lifting the deckle in the darkness, and feeling with my fingers in the pan to see ’twas full of powder.

===Chinese===

于是我伸手去够那杆枪——它就搁在我身旁的地上——撑着站起身来；在黑暗里掀起火门盖，用手指探进药池，摸一摸火药可是满的。

===Original===

The lull in the storm still lasted, and I heard the footsteps advancing, though with uncertain slowness, and once after a heavy stumble I thought I caught a muttereth oath, as if someone had struck his foot against a stone.

===Chinese===

风势的间歇还在延续，我听见脚步声一路前来，只是迟迟疑疑、走得很慢；有一回重重一个趔趄之后，我仿佛还捉着一声咕哝的咒骂，像是谁的脚撞上了石头。

===Original===

Then I shouted out clear in the darkness a “who goes there?” that rang again through the stone roofs. The footsteps stopped, but there was no answer. “Who goes there?” I repeated. “Answer, or I fire.”

===Chinese===

于是我冲着黑暗，清清亮亮喊出一声“什么人？”，喊得石顶间回声四起。脚步声停住了，却没有人应。“什么人？”我又喝了一遍。“应声，不然我开枪了。”

===Original===

“Prosper the *Bonaventure*,” came back out of the darkness, and I knew that I was safe. “The devil take thee for a hot-blooded young bantam to shoot thy best friend with powder and ball, that he was fool enough to give thee;” and by this time I had guessed ’twas Master Ratsey, and recognized his voice. “I would have let thee hear soon enough that ’twas I, if I had known I was so near thy lair; but ’tis more than a man’s life is worth to creep down moleholes in the dark, and on a night like this. And why I could not get out the gibberish about the *Bonaventure* sooner, was because I matched my shin to break a stone, and lost the wager and my breath together. And when my wind returned ’tis very like that I was trapped into an oath, which is sad enough for me, who am sexton, and so to say in small orders of the Church of England as by law established.”

===Chinese===

“愿‘Bonaventure’号兴旺，”黑暗里传回来一声，我知道自己平安了。“叫魔鬼收了你去吧，你这血气方刚的小嫩公鸡——竟要拿火药铅弹打你顶要好的朋友，那杆枪还是他傻里傻气送你的呢；”到这时我已猜出是 Ratsey 师傅，听出了他的嗓音。“我要是知道已经这样贴近你的老窝，早就叫你听出是我的声儿了；可是摸黑钻这些鼹鼠洞，又赶上一个这样的夜晚，那可是拿性命打漂的营生。至于那句带‘Bonaventure’的黑话我为什么迟迟吐不出来，是因为我拿小腿迎了一块石头，赌输了，连气也一并输掉了。等气缓回来，多半就顺嘴掉进一句咒骂里去；这于我可是桩够伤心的事，我好歹是个教堂司事，照法律立定的英国国教里，勉强也算个领了小品的人。”

===Original===

By the time I had put down the gun and coaxed the candle again to light, Ratsey stepped into the cave. He wore a sou’wester, and was dripping with wet, but seemed glad to see me and shook me by the hand. He was welcome enough to me also, for he banished the dreadful loneliness, and his coming was a bit out of my old pleasant life that lay so far away, and seemed to bring me once more within reach of some that were dearest.

===Chinese===

等我放下枪、又连哄带弄把蜡烛重新点亮，Ratsey 已经跨进洞来。他戴着一顶油布风帽，浑身水淋淋地往下淌，见了我却像是极高兴，握住我的手摇了又摇。他的到来对我也再欢迎不过：他赶散了那可怕的孤寂；他这一来，就像从我那隔得老远的老日好时光里捎来一角，仿佛又把几个顶亲爱的人，带回到我伸手可及的地方。

## XII A Funeral / 十二 葬礼

===Original===

“How he lies in his rights of a man!
						

						Death has done all death can.”

> Browning

===Chinese===

“他这样安卧着，享有一个人的名分！
死亡所能做的，死亡都已做尽。”

> Browning

===Original===

We stood for a moment holding one another’s hands; then Ratsey spoke. “John, these two months have changed thee from boy to man. Thou wast a child when I turned that morning as we went up Hoar Head with the packhorses, and looked back on thee and Elzevir below, and Maskew lying on the ground. ’Twas a sorry business, and has broken up the finest gang that ever ran a cargo, besides driving thee and Elzevir to hide in caves and dens of the earth. Thou shouldst have come with us that morn; not have stayed behind. The work was too rough for boys: the skipper should have piped the reefing-hands.”

===Chinese===

我们执手相对，站了片刻；随后 Ratsey 开了口。“John，这两个月把你从孩子变成了汉子。那天早晨，我们赶着驮马上 Hoar Head，我回过头，望见你和 Elzevir 在底下，Maskew 躺在地上——那时你还只是个孩子。那是一桩痛心的营生，拆散了天底下跑私货顶好的一伙人，还害得你和 Elzevir 躲进岩洞穴窟里藏身。那天早上你原该跟我们一道走的，不该留在后头。那活计不是孩子干的：船长早该吹响哨子，把收帆的水手全数召集上去。”

===Original===

It was true enough, or seemed to me true then, for I felt much cast down; but only said, “Nay, Master Ratsey, where Master Block stays, there I must stay too, and where he goes I follow.”

===Chinese===

这话不假，或者说当时在我看来不假，因为我心里正低沉得很；只是答道：“不，Ratsey 师傅，Block 师傅在哪儿安身，我就得在哪儿安身；他上哪儿，我就跟到哪儿。”

===Original===

Then I sat down upon the bed in the corner, feeling my leg began to ache; and the storm, which had lulled for a few minutes, came up again all the fiercer with wilder gusts and showers of spray and rain driving into the cave from seaward. So I was scarce sat down when in came a roaring blast, filling even our corner with cold, wet air, that quenched the weakling candle flame.

===Chinese===

随后我在角落的铺上坐下，觉得腿又疼了起来；歇了几分钟的风暴重新卷来，刮得愈发狂暴，一阵紧似一阵的狂风裹着浪沫和雨水，从海那面直灌进洞来。我刚坐下不久，就闯进一股呼啸的狂飙，连我们这个角落也灌满了又冷又湿的空气，把那点弱不禁风的烛火扑灭了。

===Original===

“God save us, what a night!” Ratsey cried.

===Chinese===

“上帝保佑我们，好一个夜晚！”Ratsey 失声喊道。

===Original===

“God save poor souls at sea,” said I.

===Chinese===

“上帝保佑海上那些可怜的人吧。”我说。

===Original===

“Amen to that,” says he, “and would that every ‘amen’ I have said had come as truly from my heart. There will be sea enough on Moonfleet Beach this night to lift a schooner to the top of it, and launch her down into the fields behind. I had as lief be in the Mohune vault as in this fearsome place, and liefer too, if half the tales men tell are true of faces that may meet one here. For God’s sake let us light a fire, for I caught sight of a store of driftwood before that sickly candle went out.”

===Chinese===

“对这话正该说一声‘阿门’，”他说，“但愿我说过的每一声‘阿门’，都这样字字发自我心底才好。今夜 Moonfleet 海滩上的海浪，准能把一艘纵帆船高高举过浪尖，再把她抛进后头的田地里。叫我待在这骇人的地方，我不在乎换成 Mohune 家的墓窖；倘若人们讲的、说这儿的黑暗里会迎面撞见种种脸孔的传闻有半句是真的，那我情愿去墓窖。看在上帝分上，咱们生堆火吧；那病恹恹的蜡烛熄掉之前，我瞥见那边存着好些浮柴呢。”

===Original===

It was some time before we got a fire alight, and even after the flame had caught well hold, the rush of the wind would every now and again blow the smoke into our eyes, or send a shower of sparks dancing through the cave. But by degrees the logs began to glow clear white, and such a cheerful warmth came out, as was in itself a solace and remedy for man’s afflictions.

===Chinese===

我们费了好一阵才把火生着；即便火苗已经烧得牢牢扎住了根，急风仍时不时把烟扑进我们眼里，或是掀起一阵火星，满洞飞舞。渐渐地，柴块烧出了莹白的光，散出一股欢快的暖意，本身就成了人世愁苦的一味慰藉和良药。

===Original===

“Ah!” said Ratsey, “I was shrammed with wet and cold, and half-dead with this baffling wind. It is a blessed thing a fire,” and he unbuttoned his pilot-coat, “and needful now, if ever. My soul is very low, lad, for this place has strange memories for me; and I recollect, forty years ago (when I was just a boy like thee), old lander Jordan’s gang, and I among them, were in this very cave on such another night. I was new to the trade then, as thou might be, and could not sleep for noise of wind and sea. And in the small hours of an autumn morning, as I lay here, just where we lie now, I heard such wailing cries above the storm, ay, and such shrieks of women, as made my blood run cold and have not yet forgot them. And so I woke the gang who were all deep asleep as seasoned contrabandiers should be; but though we knew that there were fellow-creatures fighting for their lives in the seething flood beneath us, we could not stir hand or foot to save them, for nothing could be seen for rain and spray, and ’twas not till next morning that we learned the *Florida* had foundered just below with every soul on board. Ay, ’tis a queer life, and you and Block are in a queer strait now, and that is what I came to tell you. See here.” And he took out of his pocket an oblong strip of printed paper:

===Chinese===

“啊！”Ratsey 说，“我刚才又湿又冷，浑身冻僵，这阵乱搅人的风差点儿要了我的命。火真是桩蒙福的东西。”他解开了领航外套的纽扣，“眼下更是缺不得。孩子，我的心绪低得很，因为这地方于我有一段古怪的旧忆；我想起四十年前——那时我也不过是个像你这样的孩子——老接货人 Jordan 那一伙，我也在其内，就在这么一个夜晚待在这个洞里。那时我初入这行当，正同你如今一样，风声涛声吵得睡不着。那是个秋天的凌晨，天还早，我躺在这儿，就躺在我们如今躺的地方，听见风暴之上响起一阵阵恸哭，唉，还有女人的尖喊，喊得我血液都凉了，到今天也没有忘记。于是我把那伙人叫醒——老练的私贩子本该睡得死沉——可我们明知脚底下沸腾的洪流里，有自己的同类正在为性命挣扎，却抬不了一根手指去救：雨里浪沫里什么也望不见，直到第二天早晨我们才得知，‘Florida’号就在底下失事沉了，船上没有一个魂灵活下来。唉，人生古怪；你如今和 Block 正落在一个古怪的窘境里，这正是我要来告诉你的。瞧这儿。”说着，他从口袋里掏出一张长方形的印刷纸条：

===Original===

G. R.

Whitehall, 15 May 1758

Whereas it hath been humbly represented to the King that on Friday, the night of the 16th of April last, *Thomas Maskew*, a Justice of the Peace, was most inhumanly murdered at Hoar Head, a lone place in the Parish of Chaldron, in the County of Dorset, by one *Elzevir Block* and one *John Trenchard*, both of the Parish of Moonfleet, in the aforesaid County: His Majesty, for the better discovering and bringing to Justice these Persons, is pleased to promise His Most Gracious *pardon* to any of the Persons concerned therein, except the Persons who actually committed the said Murder; and, as a further Encouragement, a *reward of fifty pounds* to any Person who shall furnish such *information* as shall lead to the *apprehension* of the said *Elzevir Block*, and a *reward* of *twenty pounds* to any Person who shall furnish such *information* as shall lead to the *apprehension* of the said *John Trenchard*. Such *information* to be given to *me*, or to the *governor* of *His Majesty’s Gaol* in Dorchester.

Holdernesse.

===Chinese===

G. R.

白厅，1758 年 5 月 15 日

兹经谦恭奏闻国王陛下：本年 4 月 16 日（星期五）夜间，治安法官*Thomas Maskew*在多塞特郡查尔德伦（Chaldron）教区一处名为 Hoar Head 的荒僻之地，遭该郡 Moonfleet 教区之*Elzevir Block*与*John Trenchard*二人极不人道地谋害。为便于查明并严惩此等人犯，国王陛下特予恩允：凡与本案有涉之人，除实际行凶者外，一体颁予最仁慈之*赦免*；此外为示激励，凡有人提供*线索*、据以*缉获*上述*Elzevir Block*者，赏*五十镑*；凡提供*线索*、据以*缉获*上述*John Trenchard*者，赏*二十镑*。此项*线索*，可呈报*本人*，或多切斯特*国王监狱*之*典狱长*。

Holdernesse.

===Original===

“There﻿—that’s the bill,” he said; “and a vastly fine piece it is, and yet I wish that ’twas played with other actors. Now, in Moonfleet there is none that know your hiding-place, and not a man, nor woman either, that would tell if they knew it ten times over. But fifty pounds for Elzevir, and twenty pounds for an empty pumpkin-top like thine, is a fair round sum, and there are vagabonds about this countryside scurvy enough to try to earn it. And some of these have set the Excisemen on *my* track, with tales of how it is I that know where you lie hid, and bring you meat and drink. So it is that I cannot stir abroad now, no, not even to the church o’ Sundays, without having some rogue lurking at my heels to watch my movements. And that is why I chose such a night to come hither, knowing these knaves like dry skins, but never thinking that the wind would blow like this. I am come to tell Block that ’tis not safe for me to be so much in Purbeck, and that I dare no longer bring food or whatnot, or these man-hounds will scent you out. Your leg is sound again, and ’tis best to be flitting while you may, and there’s the *Éperon d’Or*, and Chauvelais to give you welcome on the other side.”

===Chinese===

“喏——就是这张告示，”他说，“还真是一件印得漂漂亮亮的杰作；只可惜我巴不得登台的是别的角儿。如今在 Moonfleet，没有一个人知道你们的藏身之处，而且不论男人女人，就是知道了十遍，也决计没有谁会去告发。可是 Elzevir 五十镑，你那颗空南瓜脑袋二十镑，加起来是一笔足足的好数目；这一带乡下游荡的流氓里，下作得想挣这笔钱的大有人在。他们中间已经有几个把税吏支到*我*的踪迹上，说是知道你们藏在哪儿、给你们送吃送喝的正是我。闹得我如今不敢出门——连礼拜天上教堂都不成——身后不缀着个奸细窥测我的动静。我挑这么个夜晚来，也正为这个：我知道这帮家伙跟干皮子一样怕沾水；只是没料到风会刮成这副样子。我是来告诉 Block：我再在珀贝克（Purbeck）这厢频繁走动就不保险了，吃的喝的等等，我也不敢再送了，否则这些猎犬终究会把你们嗅出来。你的腿已经好利索了，趁还走得了，早挪窝为妙；对岸有‘Éperon d’Or’客栈，还有 Chauvelais 迎接你们。”

===Original===

I told him how Elzevir was gone this very night to Poole to settle with the *Bonaventure*, when she should come to take us off; and at that Ratsey seemed pleased. There were many things I wished to learn of him, and especially how Grace did, but felt a shyness, and durst not ask him. And he said no more for a minute, seeming low-hearted and crouching over the fire. So we sat huddled in the corner by the glowing logs, the red light flickering on the cave roof, and showing the lines on Ratsey’s face; while the steam rose from his drying clothes. The gale blew as fiercely as ever, but the tide had fallen, and there was not so much spray coming into the cave. Then Ratsey spoke again﻿—

===Chinese===

我告诉他，Elzevir 正是今夜去了普尔，同“Bonaventure”号的人敲定船几时来接我们；Ratsey 听了像是高兴。我有许多事想向他打听，尤其是 Grace 的近况，却又害臊，不敢开口。他也有那么一分钟没再作声，闷闷地弓着身子烤火。我们就这样蜷在角落里，守着烧得通红的柴堆；红光在洞顶上摇曳，照亮了 Ratsey 脸上的皱纹，他那正在烘干的衣裳上腾起缕缕水汽。风暴还是刮得那么凶，只是潮水已落，灌进洞来的浪沫少多了。随后 Ratsey 又开了口——

===Original===

“My heart is very heavy, John, tonight, to think how all the good old times are gone, and how that Master Block can never again go back to Moonfleet. It was as fine a lander’s crew as ever stood together, not even excepting Captain Jordan’s, and now must all be broken up; for this mess of Maskew’s has made the place too hot to hold us, and ’twill be many a long day before another cargo’s run on Moonfleet Beach. But how to get the liquor out of Mohune’s vault I know not; and that reminds me, I have something in my pouches for Elzevir an’ thee;” and with that he drew forth either lapel a great wicker-bound flask. He put one to his lips, tilting it and drinking long and deep, and then passed it to me, with a sigh of satisfaction. “Ah, that has the right smack. Here, take it, child, and warm thy heart; ’tis the true milk of Ararat, and the last thou’lt taste this side the Channel.”

===Chinese===

“John，今晚我的心沉得很，想到旧日的好时光全完了，想到 Block 师傅再也不能回 Moonfleet 了。从前那一班卸货的人手，是站得拢的数一数二的好班底，连 Jordan 船长那一伙也不能比，如今却只得散伙；Maskew 搅下的这摊祸事，把这地方烧得容不下我们了，往后许多年，Moonfleet 海滩上再别想卸一船私货。只是 Mohune 家墓窖里那些酒怎么弄出来，我是想不出法子了；这倒提醒我，我两边口袋里各揣着一件东西，是带给 Elzevir 和你的；”说着，他从外衣两边口袋里各掏出一只柳条套着的大酒瓶。他把一只举到嘴边，仰起脖子咕咚咕咚灌了个痛快，然后满足地叹了口气，递给了我。“啊，这味道才叫地道。来，拿着，孩子，暖暖你的心；这是真正的亚拉腊山奶，也是你在这边海峡能尝到的最后一口了。”

===Original===
